## Binding principles (the 13b constitution, copied per the architect-phase skill; byte-verbatim)

- **P-1 — One capability registry.** Every user-facing act is declared exactly once: verb, scope (block/take/post/book), per-mode availability rule, its doors (menu / context / bar / shortcut / palette), and its refusal copy. Surfaces render their doors FROM the registry; gating is data, never per-view if-statements. A registry-walk test asserts every declared door exists and every rendered door is declared. This kills the D3/D4 class structurally — the capability matrix stays green by construction instead of by audit.
- **P-2 — Fire-and-DO (R115).** A rendered door either performs the act — including any mode-switch choreography, done visibly for the user — or delivers the operations layer's real refusal. A door that can never act in its context isn't rendered. Lecture-and-redirect is retired.
- **P-3 — One text surface, one liveness authority.** ONE TK2 surface component (live + read modes; one measurement brain — the width-gated remeasure; the shared canary) that every pane composes; the live editor core's identity machinery (`BlockRangeIndex`, crystallization) becomes its live mode, not a sibling implementation. "What text does this pane show, and is it live" is answered by ONE pure, unit-tested authority (`PaneTextAuthority`); views never re-derive it. Its invariants stated as code: exactly one live pane; the live pane is buffer-authoritative; read panes are projection-authoritative once caught up.
- **P-4 — Broadcasts carry signals, never decisions.** NotificationCenter may announce that something happened (mode changed, post opened); no listener derives business state or availability from a broadcast, and no flow's correctness depends on broadcast ordering. Cross-feature flows route through composition. (Stage 0's survey enumerates today's notification bus; Stage 1 disciplines it.)
- **P-5 — The friction doctrine (R116).** Repeated pushback against a rule marks an under-architected feature. The response is never another patch: write the feature's DESIGN MEMO — intended behavior whole, edge cases enumerated on paper, refusal wordings, undo story — get the author's rulings on the product-flavored edges, then build to the memo. Happy path ships first, but the edges are *decided at design time*, not discovered by the author. First two memos owed: **merge** and **the W ceremony**.
- **P-6 — The tutorial is the executable spec (R113).** The guided tutorial flow is the contract for every happy path. It stays green — automated where the harness allows, screenshot-walked otherwise — and any change that breaks a tutorial step is a regression by definition.
- **P-7 — Find bugs before she does.** Every layer keeps a proactive harness: the ops-layer soaks (exist), the board/view-model invariant soak (Stage 1), the registry walk (P-1), the per-batch scripted screenshot walkthrough. Red-on-old stays absolute: no bug-class fix without a pin that fails on the pre-fix code.
- **P-8 — Standing law, consolidated.** P1–P3 performance invariants bind the rebuilt layer exactly as they bind the substrate (invalidation ∝ realized rows; one live editor; virtualized long views). Overlays, never rendering attributes. Chrome `fixedSize` texts always carry a `lineLimit` (the b6dae25 class). `Theme/VoiceControls.swift` is THE chrome vocabulary. Complexity lives in the store; restraint lives in the UI.
- **P-9 — No god objects in the frontend either.** A view model mixing board layout, text authority, and gesture routing splits by concern (`TakesViewModel` at 890 lines is the standing counterexample). Same hygiene rule as the substrate, applied where it was being waived.

*(The skill's application notes bind too: P-1's scope is the routed vocabulary grown incrementally; P-3 keeps TWO deliberate surface kinds — the full-document editor is never absorbed into `PaneSurface`, both consult `PaneTextAuthority` and the one per-post autosave coordinator; storage-coupled code stays feature-side; the overlay/sink delivery law — every equality-gated sink delivers its stored state AT WIRE TIME, and overlay geometry refreshes trigger on EVERY input axis.)*

## The members and their rulings (one line each; THE TICKET IS THE BRIEF — implementers read their tickets' full text in `notes/TICKETS.md` + Linear and the pointer records)

1. **VX-664 (High, Editor, Confirmed)** — a live pane whose take changes from OUTSIDE keeps the old words and writes them back on the next keystroke. **HER RULING (2026-10-28, on the ticket): option (a), scoped** — the ring ends ONLY when an outside write lands on the live pane's OWN take; EVERY outside-change road gets the VX-222/VX-440 ring-ending shape; **P-3's live-pane buffer authority is never bent; NO clean-buffer adoption**, now or later ("variorum is not a 'we're all editing the same text at the same time' kind of editor"). Roads to cover (the VX-440 record's W-1 general class, `notes/plans/fixes/vx-440-sheets-history-clarity.md` WALK FIXES): a Block History restore beside **Compare / Canvas / Write's take panel** (Takes + Lenses already fixed at VX-440), **a sync apply** of the take's text under a live pane (consume sync's signal UI-side only — NO `Sync/` edits), **the MCP door's `edit_take` / `restore_commit`**, **another window's restore**. The MCP flush-first rule + before/after bracket savepoints stand as collision insurance. The bound-take half is VX-248, landed at VX-601 — untouched.
2. **VX-656 (High, Editor, Suspected)** — Write's full-document editor carries VX-421's undo class: `EditorTextView` (`Features/Editor/EditorTextView.swift:880,953`) has `allowsUndo = true`, no `undoManager(for:)` under Features/Editor/, and `EditorViewModel.teardown()` (`:677`) clears nothing undo-related — typing registers on the WINDOW's manager; a mode switch dismantles the editor; a later ⌘Z pops into the freed view. Fix shape (the fix's call): the pane road (owned manager via `undoManager(for:)`) or a teardown-time history end. Pin shape: `PaneUndoLifecycleTests/undoAfterATornDownPaneIsReleasedIsACleanNoOp`. Suspected — the halves are code-read; reproduce or pin-first.
3. **VX-668 (Medium, Takes & Lenses, Confirmed)** — the Block History read-in-full sheet's Restore applies but never LANDS when the sheet was opened from the INSPECTOR (caret at block's END, no wash); the row road lands at START with the wash. **TRACE FIRST WITH THE APP** (the record's W-2 second pass, step 6: the push — `EditorView.onChange(of: pendingRestoreLanding)` — never fires on the sheet road; WHY is not observable headless). The DEBUG trace logs (a) parcel post, (b) `onChange` fire + value, (c) the settle hop, both roads. Likely fix (the architect's note, NOT pre-decided): the sink law — the editor PULLS the parcel at the settle hop after a rewrite (own-note parcels only, posted-at stamp within `RestoreLanding.patience`); the row road unchanged (its push consumes first). Pin to try: the pane mounted INSIDE PostDetailView's inspector via `requestBlockHistory`.
4. **VX-572 (High, Pre-launch, App shell)** — the ⌘/ shortcuts pane overhaul, her verbatim wants on the ticket: search; grouped under headings, **collapsed by default**; corrected spacing; the description column in the voice's face (Study's on descriptions; the chord column may stay mono); sane wrapping (no mid-name breaks in the chord column; long descriptions must not wrap into the neighbouring rhythm); easy to see which chord does what. Taste-adjacent: shots at rest in BOTH voices go in the handoff for her verdict (her sitting screenshots are the before).
5. **VX-674 (Medium, Editor)** — Block History: an UNNAMED commit's resting row carries a few words of its text as a muted preview after the stamp; a named commit keeps its name alone; the ◦ automatic mark unchanged (P-440-9's flagged gap, ruled; QUESTIONS #249).

7. **VX-676 (Medium, MCP, Feature)** — `show_in_app` lands as an OFFER. **ALL FOUR SEMANTICS SETTLED on the ticket (2026-10-28):** (1) DEFAULT = ASK, at the toast's FULL prominence (the offer kind on the status seam — it should pop up); (2) after the toast fades, an unanswered ask HOLDS the quiet line at the bottom bar's trailing end (the receipt line's home) until taken, replaced by a newer ask, or the client disconnects; the line carries its own small dismiss; the toast's ✕ sends it to the line, the line's ✕ kills the ask; NO zzz/snooze fork — the standing line IS the snoozed state; ⌥⌘O family takes it from the keyboard; (3) the dial is GLOBAL in Settings ▸ MCP — move at once · ask · never; (4) the wire answers AT ONCE, fire-and-forget ("offered — she can open it with one press"), no callback on a later take; Activity logs the ask AND the take. The dial governs FRONTMOST arrivals only; the wire answers the offered shape at once, fire-and-forget. ## Environment traps (every batch brief carries these verbatim)

- Instance checks: `pgrep -fl 'Variorum.app/Contents/MacOS'` AND discriminate by PATH (the test host matches mid-run). Real-store instances by STORE, never quit/kill one; no Release builds or /Applications refreshes while one lives (and NONE this run — no release at close).
- Mac tests from `App/Variorum/`, suite-level `-only-testing` only; confirm the "Test run with N tests" line; Swift Testing's count INCLUDES skipped tests — read the `skipped:` lines. `xcodebuild build-for-testing` compiles without the runner (stage-free). **EVERY `xcodebuild test` run needs a HELD stage claim through oct27coordinator.** Headless `swift build`/`swift test` on the core package is stage-free.
- NEVER add a custom build configuration (anything not named exactly "Debug" builds packages Release and strips the DEBUG oracles).
- zsh `pipestatus`; never `git checkout` over uncommitted work; commit per piece, push after every commit; synchronous foreground waits only — poll with `grep -q '<EXIT LINE>' <log> && break; sleep 5` loops, never `tail -f | grep -m1`.
- Walks: cliclick + `Tools/WalkKit/` on the DEV-STORE Debug build only (`io.lduarte.Variorum.debug`), under a held stage claim; screenshots in gitignored dirs; walks restore the dev store's posture.
- A1 rail: any real-store touch takes a WAL-safe UTC-named safety copy into `Backups/safety-copies/` first — but this run should never touch the real store.
- TEXTKIT2_NOTES is binding on batches 1, 2 (VX-680's geometry) and 5: no `.layoutManager` ever; attribute stamps clamped to the VERDICT's range; re-read the clip after `layoutViewport()`; read max scroll live; a frame read with `options: []` is a position only in `.layoutAvailable`; never force layout from a mid-document location on a whole-post surface.
- MCPSpec is binding on batch 3 (and any wire-called op): the grant check inside the op's transaction; one client-named ⌥⌘Z; the one not-shared shape byte-identical; `show_in_app` read-rung, never spends a once grant, never queues or raises windows (the frontmost-only refusal is RETIRED by her mid-run amendment — member 7's line; the spec sentence amends at the merge); the wire refuses undeclared arguments (VX-606); no raw Swift case in a wire answer.

#### VX-664 — THE DESIGN FORK (proposal for the architect; nothing of the mechanism is built)

**The question.** Where does "an outside write landed on take X" live, and how do the ring owners hear it — under her ruling: the ring ends ONLY when the write lands on the live pane's OWN take; P-3's buffer authority never bends; no clean-buffer adoption.

**Facts that decide it (read, not assumed):**
1. **Sync tells the UI nothing row-level.** `SyncSession` exposes a round status; the engine posts no notification and no callback names the rows a pull applied. The ONLY UI-side signal of a pull landing `working_text` is the store itself — every surface's held observation re-delivers. A shape that needs "which takes did this round write" needs a `Sync/` hook: vetoed by the rail.
2. **Every ring owner already runs ONE convergence seam on every delivery:** `authority.settle(isPending:)` — `TakesViewModel.adopt` (Takes, Lenses, and Write's take panel, which embeds a `TakesViewModel`), `CompareViewModel.adopt` (in `CompareBoardView.swift`), `CanvasViewModel.adopt`, `BlockTOCViewModel.adopt(rows:)`/`adopt(facts:)` (Read ▸ edit + the Blocks workbench), `BookIndexEditModel.settle` (called by `BookIndexView`'s per-note observation right after `rows = value`). Each holds the OUTGOING projection at that moment.
3. **The VX-440 parcel is consumed:** `TakesViewModel.consumeRestoreLanding` blurs, then the Takes board sets `pendingRestoreLanding = nil`. The router is app-wide (one `AppSession`, many windows), so a second window's board that hears the change after the first consumed it reads nil and never blurs — the "another window's restore" road is open even beside Takes today, by consumption order.
4. **The MCP receipt names no takes** (`MCPWriteReceipt`: act, target note, show) — a signal shape would need a `VariorumMCP` change to the door.
5. **Roads nobody enumerated exist** — e.g. the board's own Split at Newlines keeps the ring's block as its first half with no focus migration (`TakesStructureController.runSplit`: "the landed row keeps its identity as its own first half"), so a live pane in that block keeps every paragraph and its next keystroke writes them back (the text-duplication class). Any enumeration-based shape must find every such writer and keep finding them.

**Candidate shapes, with the P-3 / P-4 reading of each:**

- **A — one shared posted signal** (a session-level, never-consumed take-write ledger: a monotonic generation + the take ids written from outside, posted at every outside-write call site; each owner compares to its own last-seen generation). *P-4:* legal (a signal; each owner decides). *P-3:* legal. **Fails the rails/completeness:** sync cannot post without a `Sync/` hook (fact 1) — or posts a coarse "a round applied" that forces a store re-check anyway (shape B with extra steps); MCP needs receipt fields (fact 4); another window's TYPING is an outside write for this window's pane, so every autosave would have to post (keystroke-rate) or that road stays open; completeness is by enumeration (fact 5) — a standing obligation on every future writer. Not recommended.
- **C — per-road extensions of VX-440's parcel** (a parcel per road, an `onChange` per parcel per owner). Inherits A's sync veto and enumeration cost, multiplies wiring roads × owners, and keeps the consumed-parcel race (fact 3). Not recommended.
- **D — the pane SURFACE reports divergence** (on a live render, "my characters ≠ the projection text I'm handed" as a FACT, like `onFocusRequest`; the owner's authority decides live/pending and ends the ring). *P-3:* the view reports, the authority decides — legal; exact about what the pane holds. *P-4:* no broadcast. Costs: the report leaves `updateNSView` mid-update (a deferred hop — the `PanePublisher` class), a bridged-string compare per live render (TRACE 1a's cost class, one pane), ten hosts to wire (each a place to forget), and in the go-live race (below) it would END a ring she just clicked into. Viable second choice.
- **B — RECOMMENDED: the store's own delivery is the signal; the one authority decides.** At each owner's existing settle seam the owner hands the authority its live take's text in the OUTGOING and INCOMING deliveries; ONE pure rule in `PaneTextAuthority` answers *"did an outside write land on the live pane's own take?"* — true iff a pane is live, its take is in both deliveries, NOTHING of hers is pending for that take, and the incoming stored text differs from what the pane holds: her buffer where she typed since the last delivery (compared through `StoredText.stripped`, the autosave's own transform, so her own pasted U+0000 never reads as outside), else the outgoing delivery's stored text. It is computed BEFORE settle drops the buffers. On true the owner runs its own ring-end — VX-222/VX-440's shape exactly (`blur()`: its flush is provably empty, the rule requires nothing pending; the pane goes READ and adopts through the surface's existing read arm; Read ▸ edit and the book Index unmount their live-only pane, so their read-cheap rows show the stored words). *P-3:* the authority stays the ONE liveness brain and gains one rule; no view derives liveness; the live pane never adopts — it stops being live (no bend, no clean-buffer adoption). *P-4:* no broadcast at all — the held observations ARE the equality-gated sinks delivering stored state at wire time, the decision is made from stored state at delivery, and no road's correctness depends on another's ordering (fact 3's race disappears). *Rails:* sync consumed UI-side from its existing signal, zero `Sync/` lines; the MCP door byte-unchanged (flush-first + the bracket savepoints untouched); no surface, `BlockRangeIndex` or `EditorTextView` change for the rule itself. *Precedent:* 13u2 UNIT E's one structural arm (`board.noteProjectionArrived` — "makes a road nobody enumerated impossible rather than improbable"); VX-222's `endRingIfItsTakeChanged(from:)` is the take-ID twin of this text rule and sits beside it in `adopt`. VX-440's parcel blur stays, redundant by construction (VX-222/VX-227 under VX-248's precedent).

**One sub-question inside B, for the same ruling — the go-live render (B2).** Today a pane that turns live in the SAME render that carries a changed projection text keeps its old characters (`updateNSView`'s adopt arm runs only for a READ pane; the live arm writes nothing). So an outside write whose delivery shares her click's run-loop turn is invisible to B (the owner's "outgoing" text is already the new text) and her next keystroke writes the old words back.
- **B2-i (recommended):** in that transition render the pane completes its READ adoption before it turns live — it held no buffer of hers (it was projection-authoritative until this render). One editor-seam line in `PaneSurface`; not a bend of the live pane's authority, but adjacent enough to "no clean-buffer adoption" that it is the architect's word, not mine.
- **B2-ii:** leave it, recorded as B's one residual window (a delivery landing in the same run-loop turn as her click into that pane).

**Residuals under ANY shape (recorded; not this batch's to fix):**
- **(r1) An outside write landing while her typing on the SAME take is still pending (the debounce window):** the ring stays (buffer-authoritative — nothing can be adopted or ended without losing her keys or theirs), and her pending autosave lands over the outside write. MCP: the ruled insurance holds (flush-first; the AI's words are in the bracket's "after" savepoint). Restore: flushes first and is her own act. **Sync:** a pull applied into the debounce window (the local row still clean) is overwritten by her autosave and the remote words survive NOWHERE — S5's conflict-as-variant cannot see an edit not yet in the store. Pre-existing for every surface, Write's full-document editor included; a sync/autosave design question, not a ring one; unfixable UI-side without `Sync/`. **A ticket candidate for the orchestrator** (Bug · Suspected · Castle & sync).
- **(r2) A LOCK from outside on the live take** (MCP `lock_take`, another window) is not a text write — out of this class (her keystrokes then refuse at autosave; a separate class).

**Pins planned under B (built only after the ruling):** the pure rule's truth table in `PaneTextAuthorityTests` (outside write ends · her own echo doesn't · pending doesn't · her NUL paste doesn't · take absent from either delivery doesn't · nothing live doesn't); then MOUNTED red-on-old per consumer in the `RestoreLandingTests` idiom (the live pane's string still reads the pre-write words on old), each driven by a different REAL road so every family is exercised: Takes article ← MCP `edit_take` op + the board's Split at Newlines; Lenses cell ← an apply-mode `working_text` write (sync's own row write, staged through the store — no `Sync/` source touched); Write's take panel ← Block History restore; Compare ← restore; Canvas ← restore; Read ▸ edit ← MCP `restore_commit` op; book Index ← another model instance's autosave (the other-window road); the workbench ← restore. Plus (B2-i only) a surface pin: a pane turned live in the render that changes its text shows the new text.

#### VX-664 — THE SWEEP TABLE — FINAL (every road that writes an EXISTING take's `working_text` from outside a given live pane — trigger INPUTS, from the core writers outward; A-677-1: each row COVERED-by-the-seam with its named pin, or provably SAFE with the mechanism stated)

Every COVERED row is closed by the ONE mechanism at the consumer (the delivery seam), so a road is covered for EVERY consumer once its write reaches the store; the pins rotate the roads across the consumers so every road family and every consumer is exercised at least once (pins in `OutsideWriteRingEndTests` unless named otherwise; all RED on `rev664`).

#### VX-664 — as built (A-677-1 + A-677-2)

- **The rule** (`Surface/PaneTextAuthority.swift`, pure): the authority now knows what the LIVE pane holds — `liveHeld`: the text it SHOWED when it took the ring (`focus(_:showing:)`, each owner passing its own resolution — the buffer overlay over the stored text — of the list the pane's host renders), then her every keystroke (`bufferEdited`); `blur` clears it, a migration re-bases it, `.unchanged` keeps it. `settle(isPending:liveIncoming:)` settles exactly as before and answers the live pane as a SUSPECT when the delivery's stored text for its take differs from `StoredText.stripped(liveHeld)` with nothing of hers pending; with nothing known (a focus that passed no text) the delivery becomes the baseline, never a verdict. `confirmsOutsideWrite` re-applies the rule against a FRESH read.
- **Why held-tracking and not "the outgoing projection"** (a refinement inside A-677-1, found while building): (a) the Blocks workbench's model settles ONE authority from TWO lists (TOC rows and workbench facts) — after her own autosave one list's settle drops the buffer and the other list's stale "outgoing" would read as an outside write; (b) the owners that adopt deliveries DIRECTLY (Compare, Canvas, the TOC model, the book Index) can consume a photograph fetched before her own last write landed (the 13e E15 class) — so the rule judges against what the pane holds, and a fresh read confirms before any ring ends.
- **The confirmation** (`Surface/OutsideWriteConfirmation.swift`): one row by primary key, off-main (P1 ∝ one take, P2); the authority's own rule decides again against it; only then the owner's own `blur()` (VX-222/VX-440's shape; the flush provably empty). Not a broadcast — nothing posted or heard (P-4).
- **The owners** (each at its existing settle seam + each focus door): `TakesViewModel` (`adopt`; `focus`, `landAuthorityFocus`) — Takes, Lenses and Write's take panel; `CompareViewModel` (`adopt`; `focus`); `CanvasViewModel` (`adopt(face:)`; `focus(blockID:variantID:)` — `switchTake`'s re-focus rides it); `BlockTOCViewModel` (`adopt(rows:)` + `adopt(facts:)`; `makeLive` — Read ▸ edit and the Blocks workbench, the shown text read from facts where the model observes them, else rows); `BookIndexEditModel` (`settle(postID:rows:)` from `BookIndexView`'s per-note observation; `makeLive(…storedText:)` from both of the view's doors and `takePendingLiveBlock`). The old `BookIndexEditModel.settle()` stays (no production caller; pins).
- **A-677-2 (B2-i)** (`Surface/PaneSurface.swift`): the read arm's adoption body is now ONE coordinator method (`adoptProjectionText`, byte-identical steps), and `completeReadAdoptionIfGoingLive` runs it in the one read → live render whose projection text moved since the pane last adopted (asked through TRACE 1a's record — native, no foreign compare; skipped on a re-bind, which wrote already); such a render's click place is treated as a re-bind's (the landing decides — the click was made in replaced text). TRACE 1a's comment now counts four writing sites.
- **Untouched:** `Sources/VariorumCore/Sync/` (zero lines), the MCP door (zero lines), `EditorTextView` / `BlockRangeIndex` / `runStructural`, VX-248's re-bind, VX-222/VX-227's end-rules, VX-440's parcel blur (now redundant by construction), every operation (the ring-end is view state — touch, churn, receipts and undo unchanged). **MOBILE PARITY: nothing mobile-visible** (Mac-only files).

#### VX-656 — trace and fix

**The trace (my own read, the ticket's two halves confirmed; Suspected → the pins confirm on the stage):**
1. *Registration:* `EditorTextView.makeEditor()` and `makeInlineEditor()` set `allowsUndo = true`; the view's delegate is `EditorSurfaceDelegate` (`EditorView.swift`, wired by `wire(to:)` in both representables' `makeNSView`), which implemented no `undoManager(for:)` — and nothing under `Features/Editor/` did. `NSTextView` asks its delegate and falls back to the WINDOW's manager (the VX-601 probe, step 4), so every keystroke and paste registered on the window's manager, aimed at the editor.
2. *Teardown:* a mode switch removes `EditorSurface` → `dismantleNSView` → `Coordinator.uninstall()` (badges, decor, scroll anchor — no undo) and `EditorView`'s `.onDisappear` → `EditorViewModel.teardown()` (place, probe, router, canary, autosave observer, finalize — no undo). The inline editor's `uninstall` the same.
3. *So:* the window's manager kept her typing aimed at a torn-down editor; `NSUndoManager` retains no targets; the next ⌘Z with no text input focused (the window first responder) pops it into the freed view — VX-421's `EXC_BAD_ACCESS` in `popAndInvoke` — or, while something still holds the view, edits an editor no longer on screen. And `removeAllActions(withTarget:)` cannot clear TK2's registrations (VX-179 U3-g), so a teardown-time clearing of the WINDOW's manager can never be exact.

**The fix (the pane road, R-601-1's — `CommentTextArea` / `CaptureTextView` / `PaneSurface` precedent):** `EditorSurfaceDelegate` owns `undoHistory` and answers `undoManager(for:)` with it for its own wired view; `endUndoHistory()` (break coalescing + remove all) runs from BOTH coordinators' `uninstall` (the detail and the inline editor), and `EditorViewModel.teardown()` gains a belt that clears only the editor's OWN manager (never the window's — a view whose delegate already let go falls back to the window's, which is skipped). Every existing reader and history end — the structural rewrites' `textView.undoManager?.removeAllActions()`, `clearTextUndo`, the find bar's grouping, the J7 hint — resolves through the delegate's answer, so WHEN the editor ends its history is unchanged; only WHERE it lives moved. TEXTKIT2 rails: no TK1 API, nothing on the live storage, `BlockRangeIndex` and `runStructural` untouched. **MOBILE PARITY: nothing mobile-visible** (Mac-only files).

**A consequence for her (not a decision — what R-601-1's arm means on this surface):** ⌘Z in Write undoes Write's own typing only; typing in another text field of the window (the title, a SwiftUI field on the window's manager) is no longer under Write's ⌘Z, and vice versa — the same per-surface history panes have had since VX-421.

#### WINDOW vx677-b1 — PART 1 VERDICTS (the orchestrator, 2026-10-28 15:26–15:35, claim vx677-b1; logs `DerivedData/vx677-b1/logs/stage-run*.log`)

- **Run 1 (rev656): RED-ON-OLD EXACT** — "Test run with 4 tests in 1 suite failed … with 13 issues (including 8 warnings)"; the failing set is EXACTLY the three predicted (`writesTypingIsItsOwnNeverTheWindows`, `undoAfterATornDownEditorIsReleasedIsACleanNoOp`, `theInlineEditorsTypingIsItsOwnAndGoesWithIt`); the guard passed. **VX-656's revert verdict: CONFIRMED.**

- **Run 3 (post, the new pins): 34 executed, TWO FAILURES where zero were expected** — ① `OutsideWriteLivePaneTests.aLiveLensesCellShowsASyncPullToItsOwnTake` (OutsideWriteLivePaneTests.swift:227): the MOUNTED Lenses cell stays `isEditable == true` after the staged sync-pull write — the model-level twin (`lensesRingEndsWhenASyncPullWritesItsTake`) PASSES on the tip, so the authority decides the end but the mounted cell's face doesn't follow on this road in-host; ② `OutsideWriteRingEndTests.takesRingEndsWhenADivideCutsItsTake` (OutsideWriteRingEndTests.swift:204): the expectation reads `→ <not evaluated>` — `try Self.stored(db, take)` THREW before evaluating. **Both were also red on rev664 — red-on-BOTH-sides, so these two pins do not yet prove the fix for their cases (not hollow; either the fix misses the case or the pin mis-stages). Handed back to the batch-1 implementer for trace after the window; neither is a walk-row case (the walk rows' own pins are green on the tip).** Noted: a cluster of SwiftUI "Modifying state during view update" warnings around the mounted suites — for the implementer's trace (is B2-i's read-adoption writing state in a render?) and the ladder's eye.
- **Run 4 (post, the neighbours): GREEN EXACT** — "Test run with 401 tests in 49 suites passed after 127.811 seconds with 4 warnings", zero failures, zero skips. The pane family, both board families, every ring owner's suites and every undo-manager reader hold under the whole batch-1 diff.

**The two reds, traced (the batch-1 implementer, resumed 2026-10-28 after the window; headless only — nothing run on the stage):**

1. **`OutsideWriteRingEndTests.takesRingEndsWhenADivideCutsItsTake` — disposition (b): the PIN mis-staged its fixture line; the fix covers the case.**
   - *Mechanism:* `Operations.divide` stores the source take's first half through `BoundarySnapping.seamTrimmedHalves(text, cutAtUTF16:)` — the seam's whitespace TRIMMED: cutting "The second block, …" at 11 leaves the take holding `"The second"`. The pin's fixture line expected the raw prefix `"The second "` (with the space). `→ <not evaluated>` is Swift Testing's rendering of a `try` sub-expression inside `#expect`, not a throw: `stored(db, take)` read the row; the comparison was false.
   - *The evidence that the ring-end itself is red-on-old/green-on-new, from the window's own logs:* on `rev664` the test recorded TWO issues (the fixture line AND line 207 — `eventually { model.focusedPane == nil }` → the ring stayed); on `post` it recorded ONE (the fixture line only — the ring ended).
   - *The edit, line by line:* the fixture line now asserts exactly what the op stores, through the op's own public pure rule (`let firstHalf = try #require(BoundarySnapping.seamTrimmedHalves(Self.second, cutAtUTF16: 11)?.first)`; `#expect(try Self.stored(db, take) == firstHalf, …)`) — its purpose (non-vacuity: the divide rewrote her take) unchanged. Nothing else in the test moved; the structural-undo twin (which divides at the same cut) asserted no half and is untouched.
   - *Predicted:* `post` green; `rev664` red on line 207 only (one issue).
2. **`OutsideWriteLivePaneTests.aLiveLensesCellShowsASyncPullToItsOwnTake` — disposition (b): the PIN's check order raced the ring-end; the fix covers the case. One rendering fact stays OPEN (R-B1 below).**
   - *Mechanism established:* (i) on `post` the authority ENDS the ring on exactly this road — the model-level twin `lensesRingEndsWhenASyncPullWritesItsTake` (the same `deliver` → policy → fresh read → `adopt` → settle seam the mounted board runs) was green on `post` and red on `rev664`; (ii) the ring-end is ASYNC by one fresh read (`OutsideWriteConfirmation` — by A-677-1's design, so a photograph can never end her ring); (iii) on THIS host the cell reads the pulled words BEFORE any ring-end, on BOTH trees — the window's logs: on `rev664` the test recorded ONE issue (the editability line), i.e. its `shows` line PASSED with no fix in the tree; (iv) the pin waited for the text, then read `isEditable` at that same instant — before the confirmation's ring-end could land — so it was red on the fixed tree too.
   - *The edit:* the decisive check is now ONE awaited condition — the cell READ (`!isEditable`) AND showing the pulled words, within 4 s (`settle(... ) { cell.string == remoteWords && !cell.isEditable }`); the separate `shows` line is gone (green on both trees, it proved nothing on this host). Red-on-`rev664` by mechanism (the ring never ends there — the window's log already shows the cell editable on `rev664`); green on `post` predicted from (i).
   - **R-B1 — OPEN, for the next window and the ladder's eye (a rendering fact, not a clobber):** HOW the live Lenses cell came to read the pulled words while still live, on `rev664`. Only four sites write a pane's characters (`makeNSView`, the read arm, VX-248's re-bind, A-677-2's go-live completion — the last three never on a live render; `rev664` disables B2-i), so the pane the finder hit is either a fresh mount born live (a re-mount, or a second copy carrying the same identifier) — but the test logged exactly six "Modifying state during view update" warnings, in ONE 10-ms burst (15:29:06.794–.804 — one per pane of the six-block board's mount) and none between that burst and the failure, so no later mount or read adoption is visible in the log — or a road I did not find by reading. In the SAFE direction either way (a pane holding the pulled words writes the pulled words + her key), but a live cell re-mounting on deliveries would be its own defect (caret/keyboard reset while she types). The pin now PRINTS one `VX664-DIAG lenses-sync-pull:` line (panes with the identifier, whether the found pane is the same object that went live, each one's editability, the authority's liveness, `liveHeld`) — TEMPORARY, decides nothing, removed once the next window's log answers it. The Takes twin (M1, the AI's `edit_take`) shows the expected old-tree shape (its live pane kept the OLD words on `rev664`) and stays as written.
3. **The "Modifying state during view update" cluster — NOT introduced by this batch, NOT M2's mechanism.** Per test, the counts are equal on `rev664` (verdict + B2-i disabled) and `post` except exactly one extra where a read adoption ran on `post` (M1: 7 vs 6 — the read arm adopting the AI's words after the ring-end; `aPaneTurnedLive…`: 2 vs 1 — B2-i's one completed adoption). That is `PanePublisher`'s documented, designed-for publish: a pane's height is written synchronously inside `updateNSView`/`makeNSView` AND re-made on a later turn because SwiftUI may drop the first ("the ONE publish door" — round 3, MINOR-D/E/F). B2-i runs the read arm's own adoption body, so it adds the same publish the read arm always made. The pre-existing neighbour suites emitted 887 in Run 4. The verdict delivery itself writes no SwiftUI state inside a render (`OutsideWriteConfirmation` runs in a `Task` after an off-main read; the owner's `blur` mutates the `@Observable` authority outside any view update).

**Re-run expectations for the next window** (products rebuilt headless — see LIVE STATUS): **`post`, the new pins: 34 tests, 0 failures** (unchanged set — the two edited pins are the same tests). **`rev664`: 30 tests, EXACTLY 21 failures — the same 21 as before** (the divide pin still red on its ring-end line, one issue now; the Lenses pin red on its one awaited line). `rev656` and the neighbours: unchanged, no re-run needed for this hand-back (no production code changed). The `VX664-DIAG` line in the `post` run's log answers R-B1.

#### SELF-ASSESSMENT (the first of the two reviews — the implementer's)

- **The traces held.** VX-656: both halves read directly (no `undoManager(for:)` anywhere under `Features/Editor/`; neither teardown road touches undo) — Suspected → pinned (`EditorUndoLifecycleTests`), red on `rev656` predicted by mechanism. - **The one refinement inside A-677-1, stated:** the rule judges against what the live pane HOLDS (tracked in the authority from the text it showed at focus and her keystrokes), not "the outgoing delivery", and a fresh one-row read confirms before any ring ends — because the Blocks workbench settles one authority from two lists, and the direct-adopting owners can consume a pre-write photograph (13e E15). Both hazards would otherwise drop her ring WHILE SHE TYPES. Guards pin both (`aStalePhotographNeverEndsHerRing`, `herOwnTypingNeverEndsTheRing`).
- **Rails kept:** her ruling's letter — the ring ends only on a write to the live pane's own take (the rule keys on the live key's take; another take's write never ends it); P-3 whole (a live pane never adopts — `aLivePaneStillNeverAdoptsProjectionText`); no clean-buffer adoption; zero `Sync/` lines (the sync road is consumed from the store's own delivery); the MCP door byte-unchanged; `EditorTextView`/`BlockRangeIndex`/`runStructural` untouched; no operation changed (touch, churn, receipts, undo unchanged); TEXTKIT2 rails (no TK1 API, nothing new on the live storage — B2-i's write is the read arm's own body).
- **Risk to watch at the window:** the `focus(_:showing:)` baseline must be the text the pane's own host renders — every production focus door passes its owner's resolution of the list its host draws (the book Index's from the view's rows; the TOC model's from facts where it observes them, else rows). A host that rendered a different text than its owner resolves would make the baseline wrong and could end a ring on her own typing; none found by reading. The ring-end is async by one fresh read — a keystroke landing inside that gap keeps the ring (pending wins — the VX-683 class; flush-first roads are insured).
- **Consequences for her (not decisions):** after any outside write to the take her ring is in, the ring is off that pane — one click puts her back in, over the new words (VX-440's P-440-8 consequence, now on every road); after the board's Split at Newlines in the block she is in, the ring is off too. ⌘Z in Write undoes Write's own typing only (VX-656 — the same per-surface history panes have had since VX-421).

#### VX-680 — trace, design, fix

**The trace (my own read of the arrival road):** Index (her caret in block 30 of a long note) → the mode door → `BookView.switchMode` reads the departing Index's live pane (`liveCaretBlock`) → `carryPlace(from: .index, to: .outline, …)` (VX-630) parks the reveal and focuses the note AT the block (`focus(_:atBlock:)` → `caretBlockSeed`) → the Outline mounts; the focused row mounts its inline `EditorView` with that seed, and `applyFocusSeeds` puts her caret in block 30 and the keyboard in the editor — but its reveal (`revealLanding`) is inert by design on the inline surface (it has no scroll view of its own) → the Outline's `landOutlineReveal` lands the note's ROW (`page-<id>`, `proxy.scrollTo(id, anchor: .top)`) through the one landing authority. So the note's first lines show at the page top while her live block sits ~2,000 pt below the visible page.

**Design — the carried block is the landing's HOME (one landing, no second loop):** the brief's rails ("a new Outline anchor inside the live editor"; ride the existing landing road; no second scroll mechanism; the reveal minimal, not a forced top) read against what the Outline can address: the landing's acts are SwiftUI `scrollTo(id)` on ROWS, and a block is not a SwiftUI view — it is a range inside the one live `NSTextView`. So the arrival's ONE landing (the same `LandingAuthority`, same hold, quiet interval, budget, watchdog, signpost, the VX-562 follower's adoption and freeze) is told its home is the block's MINIMAL REVEAL (`LandingAim.MinimalReveal` — the Takes article's pure rule, her R-Q1), not the row's top:
- `BookViewModel.pendingOutlineBlockLanding` — a one-shot park set ONLY by `carryPlace`'s Outline arm beside VX-630's focus, taken ONCE by the Outline's landing of that note (`takeOutlineBlockLanding(for:)`), dropped by every later navigation (a focus, a collapse/collapse-all, a reveal, a block or chapter landing); `@ObservationIgnored`.
- `landOutlineReveal` takes the park and lands the row with `homingOnBlock:`; `OutlineLandingBox.blockHome` holds it for that landing only (a new landing supersedes it; the Outline leaving clears it).
- While the editor has NOT laid the block out, the landing aims and reports the ROW exactly as before (the row must realize and mount the editor first). Once it has, the target row's reports (`outlineAnchorMoved` — fired by everything that can move the block on the page: the page's scroll, the row's top, the editor's height) carry the block's residual (`OutlineBlockSighting.read` → `OutlineBlockLanding.residual`), and a correction's act is `NSView.scrollToVisible` of the block's frame — AppKit's own minimal scroll up the clip chain, the road her typing already scrolls this page by (`CommentTextArea.revealForEditing`'s precedent). One beat in practice: the row lands, the editor lays out, the next quiet look reveals the block, the hold ends it.
- At the landing's END (`finishBlockHome`, after VX-562's `deriveViewedPair`): the authority's seed table gets the row's TRUE top back (its reports carried the residual); and a landing that ended `settled`/`gaveup` with the block still off the page (it settled before the editor's geometry reached it, or the watchdog ended it) reveals the block ONCE — never after `handedover` (her scroll wins, always).
- **TEXTKIT2 rails, each kept:** no layout is forced — the block's frame is read with `enumerateTextLayoutFragments(…, options: [])` and every fragment must be `.layoutAvailable` (an unlaid one answers "not ready", never a guess; VX-246's poisoned-walk finding: never a mid-document `.ensuresLayout` on this whole-post surface — the inline editor's own whole walk lays the note out); nothing is frozen — the viewport is the enclosing clip's bounds converted at every read, and `scrollToVisible` clamps to the document's live extent itself; no `layoutViewport()` is called (so no clip re-read is owed — the landing's next look re-reads the block where it now sits); no TK1 API; nothing written to the live storage, the selection or `BlockRangeIndex` (read only); `EditorTextView`, `EditorViewModel`, `runStructural`, `PaneSurface` untouched. P1: the residual is read only for the TARGET row's reports while that landing is pending — one block's fragments plus one pass over the note's index.
- Only an INLINE editor (no scroll view of its own) under the caret for THIS note is read (`editorRouter.editing`, `scrollView == nil`) — never Write's detail editor of the same note elsewhere.
- **Rejected:** (i) a separate block landing after the row's (a second landing and a visible two-beat — the row, a hold, then a jump); (ii) `scrollTo(row, anchor: UnitPoint(y: f))` with a computed fraction (it needs the row's live height in SwiftUI's alignment arithmetic, gives no minimal reveal, and is unverifiable headless); (iii) a block anchor kind with its own policy/authority (two authorities on one surface; the follower reads one `isPending`).

**PROVISIONAL (her bundle, the orchestrator numbers at the merge):**
- **P-680-1 — a block TALLER than the page:** (a) BUILT: reveal her CARET's line inside it (her place); (b) reveal the block's top (the minimal reveal's own tall-row rule — her caret may stay below); (c) centre the caret's line.
- **P-680-2 — what "into view" means:** (a) BUILT: minimal — the block wholly on the page at the nearest edge (a block below the fold rests at the page's FOOT); (b) the block's top at the page's top (the forced top the brief rules out); (c) the comfort band (the Write landing's resting place). Her walk says whether resting at the foot reads right.
- Recorded edges, not built around: an empty block at the very END of a note answers "not ready" (TK2 has no element at the document's end — JumpPrimitive's R2-1) and the landing keeps the row's home; a caret she moves during the ~1-s hold leaves the landing homing on the CARRIED block; a width change during the arrival re-lands the top row and drops the block home (the 13u2b re-land's rule).

**MOBILE PARITY: nothing mobile-visible** (Mac-only files).

**Pins.** Pure (NEW `VariorumTests/OutlineBlockLandingTests.swift`, suite `OutlineBlockLandingTests`, 2): `theTargetIsTheBlockOrInATallBlockHerCaretsLine` (P-680-1's table), `theResidualIsTheMinimalReveal` (0 wholly on the page; the foot below; the head above; a tall target's top) — new capability, green by construction. Model + mounted (`BookNoteBoxDoorsTests.swift`, NEW suite `BookIndexToOutlineBlockLandingTests`, 3; NEW fixture `withLongNoteBook` — a 40-block first note): `theCarryIntoOutlineParksHerBlockForItsLanding` (parked by the Outline arm, taken once, a foreign note spends it, six navigations drop it, the spread and a live pane in another note park nothing) — RED on `rev680`; `aFarBlockArrivesOnThePage` (Index, block 30 live at 9 → the mode door → VX-630's premise `#require`d → block 30's laid-out frame wholly on the page and in the text view's visible rect; prints `VX-680 block 30:` + `VX-680 FAR:`) — RED on `rev680`; the guard `aBlockAlreadyOnThePageMovesNothing` (block 2 → nothing moves, the row stays at the top) — GREEN on both.

#### VX-681 — trace and fix

**The trace (my own read):** `BookView.switchMode` and `switchReadForm` both call `model.carryPlace(from:…)` BEFORE the mode changes; `carryPlace` asks `BookAnchor.landing` over `anchorPlace`; `BookAnchor.currentPost`'s `.read`/`.flow` arm returned `place.standingPostID` — where the book was last carried TO (her arrival in the flow). VX-645 had since given the flow a viewed note (`BookViewModel.viewedFlowPair`, written only by the flow's follower through `noteFlowViewed` — adopted at a landing's start, the rule while she scrolls, cleared when the flow leaves), but `anchorPlace` never handed it to the carry. So: enter at note 3, read down to note 12, switch — the next mode lands note 3.

**The fix (B4-P4, ruled in at #248):** `BookAnchor.Place` gains `flowViewedPostID` (defaulted nil, so every existing `Place` spelling is unchanged); `anchorPlace` hands `viewedFlowPair.postID`; the `.read .flow` arm answers `flowViewedPostID ?? standingPostID`. The carry reads the follower's slot AT THE SWITCH — a report read where the paged reader's address is read, never a decision (P-4); the slot is still written only by the follower and cleared when the flow leaves (which runs after the switch's carry — `switchMode` carries before the mode changes). **The empty slot falls back to the standing anchor** (she never scrolled and no geometry has reported, the flow's title holds the page, or an empty chapter's heading names no note — P-562-7) — said in a pin. The note alone is carried (the read arm's letter: no block from Read). Scroll ↔ Pages is the same carry, so the paged reader now opens at the shown story too (VX-663's fix makes the arrival land). Comments updated in `BookAnchor`'s header, `BookViewModel` (`standingAnchorPostID`, `viewedFlowPair`) and `ReadingFlowView`; A6 is not re-opened (no bar, no progress number — the follower reads geometry the landing already takes). **MOBILE PARITY: nothing mobile-visible.**

**Pins.** Pure (`BookAscentTests.swift`, `BookAnchorTests`): NEW `theFlowsPlaceIsTheStoryItsPageShows` (the viewed note wins; nothing viewed → the standing anchor; no other arm reads it) — RED on `rev681`; the table's two Read ▸ Scroll lines (`eachModeAnswersWithWhatItAlreadyKnows`) keep their assertions — a Place with no viewed note IS the fallback — with their WORDING edited (they said the flow "tracks nothing"). Model (`BookCarryTests`, on the PRE-FIX tree `7a3f5d548`): `leavingTheReadingFlowCarriesTheStoryItsPageShows` (enter 3, the follower reports 12, the carry names 12; Scroll → Pages the same) — RED on old/`rev681`; `anEmptyFlowSlotCarriesWhereSheArrived` (no report, `nothingViewed`, an empty chapter's heading → note 3) — GREEN on both; the existing `leavingTheReadingFlowCarriesWhereSheArrived` unchanged in its assertions (its doc and one message re-worded — it is the fallback now). Mounted (`BookOutlineLandingTests`, on the pre-fix tree): `leavingReadScrollForOutlineLandsTheStoryItsPageShows` (the flow entered at note 3 by its real landing, HER clip scroll of 1,400 pt to a later story — premises `#require`d: a later story clear of the last screenful — the mode door → the standing anchor, the Outline's landing, its viewed pair and the row at the top are the shown story) — RED on `rev681`; `leavingReadScrollForPagesOpensAtTheStoryItsPageShows` (`switchReadForm`'s two statements replayed → the paged reader reports the shown story) — RED on `rev681` (note 3) AND on `rev663` (page one): the family's joint leg.

#### VX-662 — trace, the fix-shape pick (written BEFORE the fix), fix

**The trace (my own read — the VX-648 FINDING confirmed statically):** `BookIndexView.select(_:scrollPane:report:)` writes `model.noteIndexAnchor(postID)` only when `report` is true — her column click (`noteRow`'s `onSelect`) and her pane tap (`BookIndexPaneNote`'s `onTap`). `landOrSeed`'s two arms both pass `report: false`: the SEED arm (no parcel — 18d batch 1's deliberate rule: a book opened straight into Index has a selected row and a nil anchor) and the CARRIED-REVEAL arm (no recorded rationale — the original W8-5 commit `46828a08d`). Nothing ever clears the anchor. `BookAnchor.currentPost`'s `.index` arm answers `indexPostID ?? standingPostID`. So: her click on A in an earlier Index visit leaves the anchor A for the BookViewModel's whole life; a later carry INTO Index of B selects B without writing the anchor; leaving Index carries A — and with B's block live the block cannot ride (`BookAnchor.landing`: live note ≠ carried note), so the Outline lands A's row.

**The three shapes, by what she would see:**

| Road | (a) the carried-reveal arm reports | (b) Index's `onDisappear` clears | (c) the `.index` arm prefers the live note |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 — the ticket's: click A earlier, carried in on B with block 2 live, leave | B + block 2 ✓ | B (via the standing anchor) + block 2 ✓ | B + block 2 ✓ |
| 2 — click A earlier, carried in on B with NOTHING live (the spread → Index carry, note only), leave | B ✓ | B ✓ | **A ✗** — no live note, the stale anchor wins |
| 3 — within ONE visit: her click on A, then an outside reveal lands D (an ascent into the open book — `requestReveal` while Index shows), leave | D ✓ (what the Index now shows) | **A ✗** — this visit's click is never cleared before the leave | A ✗ (no block rides an ascent) |
| 4 — carried in on B, then her own click on A this visit, leave | A ✓ | A ✓ | A ✓ (her click on another note's row releases the ring — `noteRow` — so no live B) |
| 5 — a book opened straight into Index (the seed arm), leave | unchanged: nil anchor → the standing anchor (18d's rule kept) | unchanged | unchanged |

**PICK: (a).** The rule already written is W21's ("the departing mode is the one that knows where she is") with `BookAnchor`'s own words for this mode: *"The index's place is its selected note (reported, like the paginated reader's address)."* (a) makes the carry anchor exactly the Index's selected note on every road that selects one — her click, her tap, and a carried or outside reveal — so the class is closed by construction; (b) and (c) diverge from (a) ONLY where they are still wrong by that same rule (roads 3 and 2 respectively), so this is not a product fork (no road shows her two defensible answers — the other two shapes leave a stale-anchor road open). The seed arm keeps `report: false` (18d batch 1's ruled reason — a cold open has no place to carry; road 5 unchanged). The one residual, recorded: a carried parcel naming a note the delivered entries do not hold falls to the seed arm and leaves an anchor from an EARLIER visit standing — reachable only if the carried note left the book between the switch and the Index's first delivery (a sync removal in that beat); not built around.

**The fix:** `landOrSeed`'s carried-reveal arm selects with `report: true` (one argument and its comment). No other line moves; `select`'s body, the seed arm, the selection report (18d) and `BookAnchor` are untouched. **MOBILE PARITY: nothing mobile-visible.**

#### VX-663 — trace and fix

**The trace (my own read; the VX-648 record's B4-6 confirmed in the code):** `PagedReadView`'s `.task` read the arrival's place (`model.pendingReveal` — W21's carry parcel, consumed in the same task — else `model.focusedPostID`), called `pages.seed(fromFocus:)`, and only THEN `await pages.adopt(entries:…)`. A fresh `PagedReadModel` is born with `entries == []`, so `seed`'s `entries.firstIndex(where:)` found nothing and left `position` at (0, 0); `adopt` then re-derived its identity anchor from the OLD (empty) entries — nil — and realized page one. Every arrival — Outline/Book/Read ▸ Scroll → Pages (`carryPlace`), Scroll ↔ Pages (`switchReadForm`), a focused note — opened at the book's first page; the reader then REPORTED post 0 (`notePagedAnchor`), so VX-563's Pages step and VX-645's Pages mark read the wrong first page until she turned. The `.onChange(of: pendingReveal)` arm cannot rescue it (the task consumed the parcel; the change it hears is to nil) and `.onChange(of: focusedPostID)` hears only later changes. The existing address pin seeds AFTER `adopt` by hand — the shape that hides the arrival.

**The fix (the ticket's "adopt with the seed"):** `PagedReadModel.adopt(entries:chapterOpeners:landingAt:)` — the arrival's place is resolved against the entries THIS adoption gives the reader, before the first `realize`, outranking the identity re-anchor (it IS where she was); a place the entries do not hold is dropped (the clamp lands a real page). The view's `.task` hands `landing ?? model.focusedPostID` into the adoption instead of seeding first. P1 kept and improved: the first realization is the carried note's spread — the book's page one is never paginated on the way (pinned). `seed(fromFocus:)` stays for a reader that already holds its book (the address pin's road); its doc now says so. VX-563's step and VX-645's mark read what the reader reports, so they inherit the fix with their pins unmodified (`theDiscretePagesMarkTheirShownNoteAndItsChapter` mounts Pages with no parcel and the focus nil or on the spread, then moves the focus — green either way; `BookNoteStepTests` are model-level). **MOBILE PARITY: nothing mobile-visible** (Mac-only files).

**Pins (`VariorumTests/PagedReadTests.swift`, suite `PagedReadViewTests`, +4; the hosted helper gains `prepare:` — what the book holds before the reader mounts, nothing by default):** `aCarriedArrivalOpensAtTheCarriedNoteNeverPageOne` (the parcel parked BEFORE the reader mounts — the real order — then the reader reports post 5 and its LEFT page draws post 5's blocks) and `aFocusedArrivalOpensAtTheFocusedNote` — both written and committed on the PRE-FIX tree (`2a636ee7b`), RED there by mechanism and on `rev663`; `theFirstAdoptionLandsTheArrivalsPlace` (the model arm: the reader IN the carried note, post 0 never sliced; a foreign place → page one) — RED on `rev663`; the guard `anArrivalCarryingNothingOpensAtTheFirstPage` (no parcel/no focus → pages 1–2; a foreign parcel → page one) — GREEN on both.

#### VX-624 — trace, confirm, fix

**The trace (my own read of `Surface/LandingAuthority.swift`, the ticket's F-1 statically confirmed):** a landing's target in a column's LAST screenful cannot reach the page's top — the column's foot (the window line, "+ new note", the padding) follows it, so at the page's maximum offset the target still stands `top` pt down. `LandingSettle.decide` knew only the target's top: outside tolerance and inside the budget ⇒ `.correct`; the correction (`proxy.scrollTo(id, .top)`) moves nothing at the page's maximum, so no report changes `top`, and the next look decides `.correct` again — every 0.25 s (the Outline's `.quiet` pace) until `elapsed > timeBudget` gives up. Invisible (a correction that moves nothing draws nothing) but real: four seconds of a PENDING landing — the VX-562 follower frozen on its adopted target, a 4 Hz loop, and a Release signpost saying `end=gaveup` for a landing that arrived exactly where the page allows. The flow (`.spacedByTheHold`, budget 6) and the Index (budget 3, a six-second watchdog) spin the same way, slower.

**The CONFIRM, pin-first (Suspected):** a headless `swiftc` driver over the REAL pre-fix `LandingAuthority.swift` + `LandingAim.swift` + `ViewportCarry.swift` (only `BookHeightManifest` stubbed — it imports `VariorumCore`; `decide` never reads it), driving `decide` exactly as `look` does against an immovable page (top 519 — F-1's measured number): **`GAVE UP top=519.0 after 16 corrections at 4.25 s`** (`App/Variorum/DerivedData/vx677-b2/logs/vx624/pre-fix-spin.log`). The class is real.

**The fix (F-1's option b′, the ticket's shape):** the page's REACH is a fact the surface hands the authority, read LIVE at every look (`reach: () -> LandingReach?` beside `mayFinish` on `land`/`watch`; TEXTKIT2 §3's "read the maximum scroll live at every use" — never a number frozen per landing). `LandingReach` is the page's room each way (`down` = max offset − offset, `up` = offset − min offset, floored at 0 — an overscroll is no room). In `decide`, a REPORTED target whose residual the page cannot close by more than the policy's tolerance (`cannotClose`; the residual's sign is the direction, `LandingAim`'s convention) is HOME: the landing finishes by the at-home rules — DONE at the hold under the surface's veto, the watchdog still binding — instead of correcting. An UNREPORTED target is never at a floor. `nil` reach (any surface that hands none) decides byte-for-byte as before. This is the same rule note Read's resting anchor has always applied through its residual's clamp (`ViewportFraction.residual`: "a resting place the page cannot reach is replaced by the nearest one it can"). The DEBUG story says `settled at the page's floor, top=…`; the signpost stays numbers-only (`end=settled top=519`).

**Every surface that consults it — the sweep (the ticket's "for every surface"):**

| Adopter | Policy | Floor shape? | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| Book Outline (`BookView.landOutline`) | `.bookOutline` | YES — F-1's traced road (the last screenful's rows) | WIRED — `reach` from `OutlineLandingBox.page.scroll` (the derived offset, the REACHABLE travel — VX-562's trace-walk arithmetic), nil until the scroll has reported in this appearance (`scrollReported`, cleared by `forgetViewedPair`, so the box's zeros never read as "no room"); pins `LandingFloorTests` (pure) + mounted `BookOutlineLandingTests.aLandingThatCannotReachTheTopSettlesAtThePagesFloorWithinItsHold` |
| Reading flow (`ReadingFlowView.land`) | `.readingFlow` | YES — a short LAST story | WIRED — `reach` from `FlowFollowBox.page.scroll` (VX-645's facts, the same arithmetic), `scrollReported` cleared by `stopFollowing`; covered by the pure pin (the flow's policy line); no mounted pin (see the self-assessment) |
| Book Index pane (`BookIndexView.land`) | `.bookIndex` | YES — a short last note, or a block near the column's end | WIRED — the EXISTING content-height observer WIDENED to `OutlineScrollFacts` (no new observer — the Outline's precedent), its old body gated on a height change exactly as before; `reach` from `PaneLandingBox.scroll`; covered by the pure pin (the Index's policy line) |
| Note Read `read`/`edit` faces | `.readNote` / `.readNoteEdit` | NO | SAFE by mechanism: their residual (`ViewportFraction`) already clamps the resting place to the reachable range — at the page's limit it IS 0 (home) |
| Takes article (`watch`) | `.takesArticle` | NO | SAFE by mechanism: the minimal reveal's home is any place where the row is fully on screen, and every row can be (a row's bottom never exceeds the content's end; a taller-than-the-page row's home is its top at the head — reachable because the content below it is taller than the page); and the typewriter room makes the last block reachable |

**Pins.** `VariorumTests/LandingAuthorityTests.swift`, NEW suite `LandingFloorTests` (5 tests): `aTargetThePageCannotBringNearerIsHome` · `theSpinAgainstAnImmovablePageEndsAtTheHold` (the authority's own decision sequence — 0 corrections, done at the hold; old: 16 and the watchdog) · `eachPolicySettlesAtItsFloorByItsOwnRules` (the flow's, the Index's; the veto holds; the watchdog still ends) — RED on `rev624`; guards `roomLeftNoReachAndAnUnreportedTargetDecideAsBefore` and `theReachIsThePagesRoomEachWay` — GREEN on both. Mounted: `BookOutlineLandingTests.aLandingThatCannotReachTheTopSettlesAtThePagesFloorWithinItsHold` (the 30-note chaptered book, the LAST note's collapsed card revealed — premise `#require`d: it stops below the top; then the DONE line says `settled` at the page's floor, ≤ 2 hops, < 2,500 ms; prints `VX-624 FLOOR:`) — RED on `rev624` (old: `gave up … ~15 hops, ~4,100 ms`). The VX-562 F-1 pins (`aLandingOnTheLastScreenfulEndsOnItsTargetNeverTheTopRow`, `aNavigatorClickOnAShortNoteInTheLastScreenfulEndsOnThatNote`) are UNMODIFIED and stay green by construction (each asserts only that the landing ENDED and the adopted target stands — never the give-up); the follower's freeze-while-travelling contract is untouched (the landing still freezes the follower while `isPending` — it now ends three seconds sooner).

**Headless twin (the pure pin's table over the REAL fixed source, `logs/vx624/fixed/main.swift`):** GREEN 21/21 (`fixed-green.log`); the behavioural revert applied to a COPY of `LandingAuthority.swift` (the `cannotClose` arm → `|| false`) — RED on exactly the 8 floor lines, the sequence back to `giveUp after 16 at 4.25` (`revert-red.log`). The tree was never reverted.

**THE USE WALK — batch 2 rows** (an Opus walker under the orchestrator's claim; the DEV-STORE Debug build; both voices where a face shows; one capture per row). The dev store holds no book: the window's first act is Help ▸ Restore the Sample Library (the seeded Sherlock — 13 long one-note chapters) and its last is VX-562's removal routine (Delete Book and Its 13 Notes; the Welcome shelf; the `classics` / `sherlock holmes` tags), prefs export/diff by exact key, Resume never pressed.
- **W-680-1 — Index → Outline lands block-precise (her acceptance road):** Index: click into a paragraph FAR down a story (≥ 30 paragraphs in — "A Scandal in Bohemia"), mid-word; the mode tab Outline → the story arrives LIVE at that paragraph with the caret where she left it (VX-630) AND the page shows that paragraph — the note's row lands first, then (within about a second) the page moves the minimum that shows the paragraph, which rests at the page's FOOT; type one key → it lands at her caret. Capture the Index before and the Outline after. Both voices. **For her eye (P-680-2):** does the paragraph resting at the foot read right, and does the short two-beat (row, then paragraph) read calm?

- **W-662-1 — the stale anchor, live:** Index: click story A's row in the column; mode tab Outline; in Outline click into story B's second paragraph; mode tab Index (B's paragraph live there — VX-304); mode tab Outline → B at her paragraph — NEVER A. Both voices.
- **W-681-1 — leaving Read ▸ Scroll from the story on screen:** Read ▸ Scroll, arrive at story 3 (its navigator row), wheel down into story 8 (the navigator's mark on 8); mode tab Outline → the Outline lands story 8 (the navigator's mark and the pinned line "note 8 of 13 · …"), never 3. Both voices.

#### SELF-ASSESSMENT (batch 2 — the first of the two reviews, the implementer's)

- **The traces held, each read in the code before a line was written:** VX-624 — confirmed on the REAL pre-fix `decide` by a headless driver (16 corrections, give-up at 4.25 s); VX-663 — the `.task` seeding a reader with no entries (the B4-6 finding exactly); VX-662 — `select(report:)`'s two silent arms, the anchor never cleared (statically — the stage's `rev662` run is the confirm, and the record says what to do if it is green); VX-681 — the `.read .flow` arm and an `anchorPlace` that never handed the flow's slot; VX-680 — the inline editor's inert reveal and a landing that knows only rows.
- **What each pin is worth:** VX-624 and VX-681 have headless twins over the REAL source files, red/green both ways (the reverts applied to COPIES — the tree was never touched for them). Every Mac pin is compiled only: its red on the revert product and its green on `post` are the window's. The pre-fix-tree commits (`2a636ee7b` VX-663, `97384209d` VX-662, `7a3f5d548` VX-681) carry the pins BEFORE their fixes.
- **Where I would want the cold reviewer to stare:**
  1. **VX-680's home-switch inside the authority** — the target row's reports carry the block's residual (a minimal-reveal number) on a policy whose declared aim is `.top`; the declared kind still answers the row's top for every other landing (the agreement pin is untouched), and the end-of-landing re-seed restores the row's true top so no later landing seeds from a residual. A reader who sees `top=` in the DEBUG story for that landing is reading the row; `block-home residual=` is the number the authority was given.
  2. **VX-680's aim is AppKit's `scrollToVisible`, not `proxy.scrollTo`** — the one place an Outline landing moves the page through the clip rather than SwiftUI; justified (a block is not a SwiftUI view) and precedented (the caret road; `CommentTextArea.revealForEditing`), but it is the line most likely to be read as "a second scroll mechanism". The VX-562 pins already prove a programmatic clip scroll holds on this ScrollView (no `.scrollPosition` binding).
  3. **VX-680's editor choice** — `editorRouter.editing` filtered to this note and an inline editor. If a SECOND inline editor of the same note (another window's book on the same note) took `editing` during the ~1-s hold, the sighting would read that editor's geometry; recorded, not built around.
  4. **VX-624's reach on the Index** — the Index's height observer was WIDENED (the action now fires on offset changes too and gates its old body on a height change); equivalent by construction, but it is a Release observer whose firing cadence changed.
  5. **VX-662's pick** — the carried reveal now writes the carry anchor; the seed arm still does not (18d's cold-open rule). The table in the VX-662 section is the argument that this is not a product fork.
  6. **VX-663's `landingAt:` outranks the identity re-anchor** in `adopt` — only the FIRST adoption of a reader is ever handed a place (the view's `.task`); the `.onChange(of: entries)` adoptions pass none, so a membership change mid-read still re-anchors by identity (`theAddressSurvivesMembershipAndOrderChangesByIdentity` unchanged).
- **Rails kept:** zero lines under `Sources/` (the whole branch: `git diff origin/main...HEAD -- Sources Package.swift` is empty); no `Sync/`, no custody, no schema; `EditorTextView`, `EditorViewModel`, `BlockRangeIndex` (read only), `runStructural`, `PaneSurface` untouched; TEXTKIT2's rails on VX-680 each kept (stated in its section); no new dependency; no literal colour, no copy change. **MOBILE PARITY: nothing mobile-visible** for all five (Mac-only files).

**Work from `~/dev/variorum-vx-677` (branch `vx-677-run-editor-panes-the-ai-door`; pull first) under batch 1's exact header rules: headless only, `build-for-testing` into a fresh `App/Variorum/DerivedData/vx677-b3/`, NO `xcodebuild test`/app launch/walk/stage, no subagents, commit+push per piece with LIVE STATUS in a new BATCH 3 RECORD section from the first commit, foreground waits only, NO attribution trailers. #### VX-675 + VX-676 — the design as built (the rulings are the spec; the member lines quote them)

**The one machinery (every target rides it — note, takes, lens, book, wing, compare, version):** the wire decides READ on the target exactly as before (never spending; the ONE not-shared shape for anything unreachable), reads the DIAL in that same read (`Queries.mcpShowDial` — the local-only `mcp_switch` key `show_dial`, no migration, never synced: A-6's boundary-state home; absent or unknown reads as the ask), and hands the app ONE `MCPShowRequest` (target · client · the decision's titles · compare's peers' titles · the dial · the Activity target). The app applies the ONE pure rule on the main actor in the delivery's own hop — `MCPShowRule.verdict(standing, dial:)`: **never → refuse** (the never sentence) · **ask → offer** · **move at once → navigate only `.frontmostMainWindow`, else offer** (her amendment: never refused for where she stands; a background arrival is the ask under every dial value but never — RATIFIED). The frontmost-only refusal words are deleted. Nothing in either road orders a window: an offer touches only the session's status seam; her TAKE (her own press) opens through `creationRouter`, the receipt's Open › precedent.

**The standing ask (her ruling (2) as settled):** `StatusCenter.stand` puts the ask on the toast at full prominence (the offer kind — under every receipt style) AND in a new `standing` slot; the toast's own clock fades only the toast; `lineItem` (the quiet line's one rule) shows a quiet receipt while one speaks, else the standing ask unless the toast is showing it. The toast's ✕ is the shell's `clear()`, which never ends a standing ask — so it "sends it to the line"; the line draws the ask's Open › and its own small ✕ (`Item.dismiss`), which ends it. It ends when taken (either Open ›, or ⌥⌘O — `showLastChange` takes a standing ask first), replaced by a newer ask (one slot), its client disconnects (`adoptConnected` — no live session left under its name; a name's sessions count as one), or the library closes (detach). The Library's message row, the Pool's foot and the book page foot — the three homes that gate on the slot — now gate on `lineItem`, so the ask holds the line wherever the receipt line lives. Activity: the ask is its own row (`show_in_app_offered`, logged by the wire), her take its own (`show_in_app_taken`, logged by the app off-main under the asking client's name on the ask's target); a move keeps `show_in_app`'s row; a dismissal logs nothing. `list_my_activity` lists the ask as `show_in_app` + `offered: true` (with the reads), never the take — so polling can't call her back (fire-and-forget held on every road).

**VX-675:** `target: "version"` + `note_id` + `version_id` (read_note's pair) — the NOTE's read decision; `Queries.isVersionHistory(version, of: note)` false answers the ONE not-shared shape decided over no facts (byte-identical to an unreadable note's: pinned against a random id, the note's own id, a live note, another note's version, a promoted version, a hidden note's real version); `version_id` with any other target refuses in words before any read. The app lands it through the shell's one door as VX-440's "Open full size" does (`.noteVersion(versionID, of: noteID)` — the band, Back, Promote, read-only, no per-note memory: P-440-1 holds by the destination's own construction).

**Rails, each kept:** grant check inside the decision's read (a read-rung tool — no transaction writes but the log); read rung, no once spend (pinned on the note, the version, compare); never raises a window; the ONE not-shared shape byte-identical; VX-606 (the new `version_id` and `"version"` are in the published schema; an undeclared argument still refuses — pinned); no raw Swift case on the wire (the ask's act key never reaches `list_my_activity` raw — pinned); Activity in words (both new rows worded — pinned); Private/Seal/Own untouched; zero `Sources/VariorumCore/Sync/` lines; no schema change. **MOBILE PARITY: nothing mobile-visible** (the MCP door is Mac-only; the shared `MCPShowDial`/`MCPShowActivity` are core types the phone never reads).

#### SELF-ASSESSMENT (batch 3 — the first of the two reviews, the implementer's)

- **The traces held.** VX-675's "deliberate refusal" was not a sentence but a fact: `Queries.mcpNoteFacts` excludes born-archived versions (`archived_at = created_at`), so a version id as `note_id` answered the one not-shared shape; the new target decides on the NOTE and asks `isVersionHistory` after — read_note's own road. VX-676's old etiquette was one pure verdict (`MCPShowRule.verdict(standing)` → navigate | refuse) applied in `MCPDoor.agentShow`; the offer is that verdict growing a dial and an `.offer` arm, plus a standing slot on the one status seam.
- **What each pin is worth:** the wire pins ran headless here both ways (red on each revert applied to the tree, green after — "Headless twins"); every Mac pin is compiled only — its red on `rev676`/`rev675` and its green on `post` are the window's, with the exact expected failures listed. Two members' pieces are new capability with no old behaviour to be red against (the dial's store row, the Settings row, the words, the registry doors) — their pins are GUARDS, said so per pin.
- **Where I would want the cold reviewer to stare:**
  1. **`StatusCenter.standing` and `clear()`.** The toast's ✕ is the generic `clear()`; it never ends a standing ask — that is what makes "the toast's ✕ sends it to the line" true by construction — but it means EVERY `clear()` in the app (a door's own pre-act clear, `QuietReceiptLine`'s buttons) leaves the ask standing. Only `endStanding` ends it, and only `MCPDoor` calls it (take, dismiss, replace, disconnect, detach). A future caller who wants "silence everything" would miss it — named here, not built around.
  2. **One message at a time, bent once.** `deliver` still clears the other slot; the standing ask is a third thing beneath both (`lineItem`), so a refusal toasting while an ask stands shows the ask on the line at the same time (two places, two messages). Deliberate — the ask must stay reachable — but it is the first exception to 18i's "at most one of the two is ever non-nil" comment, which stays true of `current`/`quietLine`.
  3. **The dial is read twice, from one store.** The wire reads it in the decision's own read (authoritative for the call); the pane's picker reads the snapshot. A dial flip landing between a call's read and the app's verdict is decided by the wire's read — the call's own moment.
  4. **P-676-4 (a move she leaves mid-way becomes the ask)** is app-side, in `agentShow`'s `.interrupted` arm, which is why `rev676` reverts two lines, not one.
  5. **The disconnect end and one-shot clients.** A client that calls `show_in_app` and hangs up at once ends its own ask within a moment (her rule — "or the client disconnects"); the walk's driver must stay connected (said in the rows). Claude Code's session keeps its connection, so the ruled shape is the everyday one.
  6. **`list_my_activity`'s entry grew one field** (`offered: true`) on ask rows only; the tool name is mapped back to `show_in_app` so no non-tool act ever reaches the wire raw; her take is excluded by the act list. Every other entry is byte-unchanged (the existing suite green).
  7. **The never dial is decided AFTER the target's read decision** (P-676-2) — an unreadable target still answers the one not-shared shape under never (pinned), so the never sentence is only ever said about a target the client can read.
  8. **The line's three gated homes** (the Library's message row, the Pool's foot, the book page foot) now gate on `lineItem` instead of `quietLine` — a standing ask holds them as a receipt would; the optional run 5 re-checks the book surfaces.
- **Rails kept:** zero lines under `Sources/VariorumCore/Sync/`, no custody, no schema (a key in the local-only `mcp_switch` family; no migration); the grant check is the decision's own read (read rung, no once spend — pinned three ways); never raises a window (an offer touches only the status seam; her take uses `creationRouter`, her own press's rule); the ONE not-shared shape byte-identical for every unreachable version id (pinned against six forms); VX-606 (the new argument and target in the schema; an undeclared one refuses — pinned); no raw Swift case or raw act on the wire; Activity in words for both new rows; Private/Seal/Own untouched; the instructions 3,061 of 3,100 (the allowance not used); P-1 (the two doors declared `.surface`, the walk's new line; the dial a catalog row). **MOBILE PARITY: nothing mobile-visible.**

#### FINDINGS RECORDED, NOT FIXED (batch 3 — for the orchestrator to ticket or dismiss)

- **F-B3-1 (Test-only · Tests & tooling — suggest HIGH: it makes pins lie in both directions).** On this toolchain (Apple Swift 6.4, swiftlang-6.4.0.34.1 — Xcode 27.1), inside a NESTED closure (the `withOperations { ops, db in … }` / `withSession { … }` shape most pins use), a Swift Testing macro wrapping a closure-bearing expression — `#expect(try db.read { … } == …)` or `#require(try db.read { … })` — makes a LATER closure of the same type in the same enclosing closure run the FIRST closure's body. Repro (minimal, deterministic, 1 of 1 runs): `try withOperations { _, db in let first = try #require(try db.read { try String.fetchOne($0, sql: "SELECT 'first'") }); let second = try db.read { try String.fetchOne($0, sql: "SELECT 'second'") }; #expect(second == "second") }` → `second == "first"`. The same two lines in a plain function body (no enclosing closure) behave; a later closure of a different type behaves. Mechanism (inferred, not proven): the macro-expanded closure and the enclosing closure's next closure get the same mangled discriminator, so one body is emitted for both. Found when this batch's own core pin went red for a reason its query could not produce (the take's row came back from a filter that excludes it — it was the PREVIOUS read's body); bisected to the shape above with throwaway probes (removed; copies in this session's scratch). **Exposure:** 161 `#require(try … .read {` sites in 71 test files (core + Mac), plus every `#expect(try … .read {` — any of them followed by a same-typed `read { }` in the same closure reads the wrong thing; a pin asserting the second read can then pass hollow or fail falsely. Not swept here (out of this batch's unit); this batch's own pins bind every read to a local before asserting.
- **F-B3-2 (Suspected · MCP, suggest Low):** a write receipt's "Open ›" (`MCPDoor.openShow`) silently does nothing when no FULL window exists (`creationRouter()` nil — every full window closed, a note-only window open): a door that did nothing, speaking nothing (P-2). Pre-existing; this batch's TAKE of an ask says so in words and keeps the ask standing, but `openShow` (the receipt's road and ⌥⌘O's last-change arm) was left as it was — one line to align at the next touch.
