#### D-1 — anomaly A: the hollow mounted Lenses pin (`OutsideWriteLivePaneTests.aLiveLensesCellShowsASyncPullToItsOwnTake`)

**The mechanism, PROVEN from the compiled test binary (headless — `nm` + `objdump` over the products that ran; not inferred):** the pin's decisive predicate is NOT in the binary. The test body (`closure #1`, the `withWorld` closure) holds three `() -> Bool` predicates handed to `settle(_:_:timeout:until:)`: (c1) `#expect(await Self.settle(window, hosting) { pane("cell")?.string == prose(2) })`, (c2) `#expect(await Self.settle(window, hosting) { pane("cell")?.isEditable == true })`, and (c3) the plain `let ended = await Self.settle(window, hosting, timeout: 4) { cell.string == remoteWords && !cell.isEditable }`. The binary carries only TWO `() -> Bool` closure bodies in that context: `closure #5` (calls `pane`, `string`, `prose(2)` — c1) and `closure #11` (calls `pane`, `isEditable` — c2). The three `settle` call sites (async partials `TY3_`, `TY5_`, `TY9_`) hand it `partial apply for closure #5` at 0x325fb64, `partial apply for closure #11` at 0x325fb74, and — for `ended` — a SECOND `partial apply for closure #5` (`…fU3_TA.47`, LLVM's uniquing suffix) at 0x325fbf8, which branches to closure #5's body at 0x325d580. c3's body (the only code that would read `remoteWords` and `!isEditable` in a predicate) is never emitted: the macro-wrapped c1 and the plain c3 received the SAME closure discriminator (#5) and one body was emitted for both. Identical in all three products that touched it (`vx677-b2/post` 17:10, `vx677-b1/post` 16:13 and `vx677-b1/rev664` 16:15 — leg 1's two runs used the vx677-b1 pair). **This is VX-692 (F-B3-1) itself, now shown at the machine-code level rather than inferred:** a closure-bearing expression inside a Swift Testing macro shares its discriminator with a later same-typed closure in the same enclosing closure, and the later site runs the earlier body.

**So what the pin actually asserted, on both trees:** "the cell still reads the pre-pull words" (c1's body) — true at the FIRST look on BOTH trees, because the live cell is buffer-authoritative and keeps `prose(2)` until a ring-end (none on `rev664`; on `post` the ring-end is one confirming read away). That is every observed fact: the pass in ~0.25 s on both trees (vx677-b2 leg 1: 0.253 s `rev664`, 0.235 s `post` — no wait ran), and the DIAG printed right after it reading `editable=[true] authority-live=true` on BOTH trees (nothing had happened yet; on `post` the ring-end had not landed in the first look). The Takes twin in the same file is compiled honestly — its three predicates are three bodies (#2 `prose(3)`, #8 `isEditable`, #3 `aiWords`) — which is why it behaved (4.4-s timeout on `rev664`, 0.4-s pass on `post`).

**R-B1 — ANSWERED (closed):** the live Lenses cell NEVER read the pulled words while live, on either tree. vx677-b1's original form (`let shows = await Self.settle(...) { pane("cell")?.string == remoteWords }`, then `#expect(isEditable == false)`) had the same closure set in the same order, and its run is the same signature by timing: on `rev664` the test ended in 0.276 s with only the editability issue — a real `remoteWords` predicate there would have run its 4-s timeout (as the Takes twin's does, 4.4 s). That form's binary no longer exists (rebuilt), so for it the merge is shown by timing + identical shape; for the corrected form it is shown in the binary. The remount theory was already dead (vx677-b2's DIAG: one pane, the same object); the "road I did not find by reading" is the compiler, not the app. No app defect stands behind R-B1: the four writing sites in `PaneSurface` are the whole list.

**What the evidence of record for VX-664's sync-pull road is, today:** the model-level twin `OutsideWriteRingEndTests.lensesRingEndsWhenASyncPullWritesItsTake` (red on `rev664`, green on `post` — exact in BOTH windows; its body has no closure-bearing macro shape on the asserted read) plus the walk's W-664-1/2 on film (the ring-end and the read pane's adoption, live, on the MCP and restore roads). The mounted Lenses pin has proven nothing in either window.

**The binary-level detector (a lead for VX-692, not a sweep):** an explicit closure's partial-apply forwarder carrying an LLVM uniquing suffix (`…fU<n>_TA.<k>`) marks a site where two source closures share one name. Over the whole `vx677-b2/post` test binary (`nm | grep '^_\$s13VariorumTests' | grep -E 'fU[0-9]*_TA\.[0-9]+$'`) it finds exactly TWO such sites: this pin's, and `MCPShareFacesTests.theIndicatorSaysOnlyWhatIsTrue()`'s `closure #2` (F-B3-1's exact `#expect(try await db.read {…})`-then-`db.read {…}` shape). It sees CAPTURING closures only — a merged non-capturing closure (F-B3-1's own repro) emits no thunk to duplicate — so it is a lead list for VX-692's sweep, never a census. Nothing outside this unit's three items is touched.

**The disposition: REPAIRED (not retired) — the pin's logic was sound; its compiled form was not.** Once every predicate is its own body, what was written holds: on `rev664` nothing ends the ring, the live cell keeps the old words (a live pane never adopts — P-3), so the awaited condition waits out its 4-s timeout → RED; on `post` the model-level twin proves the authority ends the ring on this exact road and the mounted Takes twin proves the read pane then adopts the stored words → GREEN predicted (PENDING the window — no honest run of this mounted cell exists in either window, since both ran the merged form).

**The repair, line by line (`a9dfdf806`, `OutsideWriteLivePaneTests.swift`):**
1. `let column = try #require(model.columns.first { $0.kind == .main })` → `let mainColumn = model.columns.first { $0.kind == .main }` + `let column = try #require(mainColumn)` — the closure leaves the macro (same value; the merge rule needs a closure inside a macro).
2. `#expect(await Self.settle(window, hosting) { …string == prose(2) }, "fixture: …")` → `let showsTheTake = await Self.settle(…) { …same body… }` + `#expect(showsTheTake, "fixture: …")` — the first fixture predicate, the one whose body the decisive `settle` had been running, is now a plain closure.
3. The same for the LIVE fixture (`let isLive = …` + `#expect(isLive, …)`).
4. NEW `let stored = try await storeText(w, take)` + `#expect(stored == remote, "fixture: the pull's write stands in the store")` — non-vacuity, as the Takes twin has it (`storeText` is a plain function; no closure at the call site); green on both trees by construction (an SQL write). It separates "the ring did not end" from "the staged pull did not land" on a red.
5. The decisive `ended` predicate: body BYTE-UNCHANGED.
6. The final `#expect(ended, …)`'s message reads the pane once into `cellNow` (no behaviour change; the message only).
7. The comment block that claimed "on this host the cell already reads the pulled words BEFORE that ring-end, on both trees" is REPLACED — that claim was the merge's illusion — by the true mechanism on each tree; the test's doc gains a paragraph saying why closure-bearing expressions stay out of the macros here.
8. The DIAG helper and its call (`d1f72f488`, its own commit): gone.

**The rebuilt binary says what the source says (`vx677-b1/post`, 20:15, `build-post-7.log`):** the Lenses test's explicit closures are now #1 (`kind == .main`), #2 (`string`, `prose` — fixture 1), #3 (`isEditable` — fixture 2), #4 (the apply-mode write), #5 (`string`, `isEditable`, `remoteWords` — the decisive predicate), each with ONE thunk; the three `settle` sites hand it `partial apply for closure #2` → body #2, `#3` → body #3, and (`TY11_`, `ended`) `#5` → body #5. The whole-binary detector now finds only the MCP lead above.

#### D-2 — anomaly B: the 97-pt guard (`BookIndexToOutlineBlockLandingTests.aBlockAlreadyOnThePageMovesNothing`)

**F-B3-1 checked FIRST — RULED OUT.** The failing read is `BookOutlineGeometry.anchorTops[BookOutlineRows.Row.noteAnchor(x)]` — a static dictionary subscript, no closure — and the test body holds no closure-bearing expression ahead of it that could donate a body (its only explicit closure is the `top.map { abs($0) <= 4 }` inside the failing `#expect` itself; the helper `indexBlockToOutline` puts no closure inside a macro). The binary agrees: no duplicated thunk in the guard or its helper (`vx677-b2/post`). And the value is corroborated independently by the APP's own landing trace in the same run — `block-home residual=0 row-top=97`, printed by `BookView.outlineAnchorMoved` from the row's own geometry report. 97 is the row's true top; the pin reads what is there.

**The mechanism (traced from the window's own landing stories — `stage-b2r6-post61.log`, `stage-b2r3-rev662.log`, `stage-b2r5-rev680.log` — and `BookView.swift`):**
1. The arrival lands note 0's row (the long note, the column's FIRST row, 97 pt below the page's top at offset 0 — the column's head above it). The LAND act scrolls it to the top: the row reports `top=97.0`, then `top=0.0`, `top=0.0`.
2. A CONTENT COLLAPSE follows: while the inline editor replaces the card the row measures `h=101`, then `h=99`, and the whole column's content reads `content=482` / `480` — SHORTER than the page (774 pt) — so the scroll clamps to offset 0 and the row falls back to `top=97.0`.
3. The editor lays the note out (`h=3029`, `content=3410` — the page can scroll again) and its block 2 is laid out. From that moment `outlineAnchorMoved` (BookView.swift:1941–1948 on the pre-fix tree) reports the BLOCK's minimal-reveal residual INSTEAD of the row's top: `block-home residual=0 row-top=97` — block 2 is on the page, so the residual is 0.
4. The authority sees its target at home: no correction; `DONE: settled at top=0.0 after 0 hops` — the `0.0` is the block's residual, not the row (the row is at 97). The page never moves again; the guard reads 97.
- **On `rev680`** (no block park → no block home) the SAME first three steps run (`top=97 → 0 → 0 → [collapse] → 97 …`), and the authority — hearing the row — corrects: `HOP 1 at 380 ms (top was 97.0)` → `top=0.0` → `DONE: settled at top=0.0 after 1 hops`. The guard passes there.
- **The far-block pin meets the same drift** (`aFarBlockArrivesOnThePage`, post61: `block-home residual=1660 row-top=97` → one reveal → `residual=0 row-top=-1563`): it hides it, because its block's own reveal moves the page anyway.

**So it is a REAL defect, not a pin artifact:** VX-680 removed the landing's drift correction for the row whenever the carried block happens to be on the page — a regression of the Outline landing's standing contract (the row lands at its top, corrections included, until the hold), in exactly the case VX-680 did not mean to change. The design's own words say the row comes first: P-680-2(a) "reached inside the arrival's one landing (**the row first**, the block within about a second)"; the batch-2 design "One beat in practice: the row lands, the editor lays out, the next quiet look reveals the block"; and the guard's contract "the page does not move for it — the row stays at the top". The code's hand-over (row → block residual the moment the block is laid out, whatever the row is doing) is what diverges.
- **In-host vs live:** the walk's W-680-2 (Sherlock, 13 long notes, total ~36,000–42,000 pt) never clamps — no card's transient can make that column shorter than the page — so the row stayed home and the probe saw it right. The harness shape (a SMALL book: three notes, the long one first; the column briefly shorter than the page while the editor mounts) is an ordinary book of hers, and the same drift is reachable by any layout settle that moves the row while the block stays visible. Not verified live (no stage this session).

**The fix (the design's own sequence, nothing new):** while the carried block's reveal target would be wholly on the page WITH THE NOTE'S ROW AT THE PAGE'S TOP, the landing's home is the row's top — the Outline landing exactly as it always was, its act the row's `scrollTo(.top)`; only a block that would NOT fit below the row homes on its own minimal reveal (VX-680 as built, byte-identical numbers — the far block's `1660` and its one reveal). One pure rule, `OutlineBlockLanding.homeIsTheRow(targetTop:targetHeight:rowTop:viewportHeight:)` — the minimal reveal's own rule (`LandingAim.MinimalReveal`) asked of the target placed below the row's top — consulted at both of the landing's points that must agree: the report (`outlineAnchorMoved` notes the row's top or the block's residual) and the act (the row's `scrollTo` or the block's `scrollToVisible`). It reads only the sighting VX-680 already takes (no new layout read — TEXTKIT2's rails untouched) and the row's top the same report carries. Oscillation-free: which home applies depends on the block's place INSIDE the row (a layout fact), never on where the page stands, so no act can flip it. The end-of-landing net (`finishBlockHome`) is unchanged.

**As built (`00c07d55d`):** `OutlineBlockLanding.homeIsTheRow` (pure) + `OutlineBlockSighting.homeIsTheRow(rowTop:)`; `BookView.outlineAnchorMoved` notes the row's top when it answers true (the DEBUG trace line gains ` row-first`), else the block's residual as before; `landOutline`'s act reveals the block only when it answers false for the row's latest reported top (`box.page.tops[id]`, written by the same report before any act reads it), else the row's `scrollTo(.top)`. Untouched: the park (`BookViewModel`), the sighting's read (no new layout read — TEXTKIT2's rails as VX-680 stated them), `finishBlockHome`, `LandingAuthority`, every other landing. **P-680-2 is not re-opened** — (a) as built stays her bundle's question; this makes the code do what (a)'s own words say. **MOBILE PARITY: nothing mobile-visible.**

**Pins.** (1) The mounted guard `aBlockAlreadyOnThePageMovesNothing` — UNCHANGED in its body; it is this defect's red-on-old pin, already observed RED twice on the pre-fix code (vx677-b2's post61 and rev662, `row-top=97`) and GREEN on `rev680`; its doc's "Green on both" (never true with VX-680 active) is corrected to say so. Predicted on the next window: GREEN on `post`, RED on `revB` (the same 97). (2) NEW pure `OutlineBlockLandingTests.theRowComesFirstWhileTheBlockFitsBelowIt` (the four "row's top" lines + three "block's own reveal" lines + the far block's residual, every value bound to a local — VX-692): its headless TWIN over the REAL source (`swiftc` over the `OutlineBlockLanding` enum extracted from the file + the real `LandingAim.swift` + `ViewportCarry.swift`; `DerivedData/vx677-b2/logs/d2/`) — the tip GREEN 8/8 (`fixed-green.log`); the revert applied to a COPY (`homeIsTheRow` → `false`) RED on exactly the 4 row-first lines (`revert-red.log`). The far-block pin's numbers are unchanged by construction (its block never fits below the row: `homeIsTheRow` false at `1660` and at `0`).

#### D-3 — F-B2W-1: the Study mis-landings (walk vx677-b2, W-662-1 Study)

**What the facts ESTABLISH (from the record's signposts, the captures `.vx677-walk-shots/b2/W662-1-S-*`, and the code — no stage):**
1. **The landing truly settled; the page moved AFTER it ended.** Both bad signposts say `hops=0 … end=settled top=0` — zero corrections, i.e. the target row reported within tolerance from the first look through the whole ~1-s hold. The capture `W662-1-S-b-outline-A` shows the breadcrumb and the navigator's mark on **note 10** — the VX-562 follower's derivation, which is FROZEN while a landing is pending and at the landing's END keeps the adopted target if its row is on the page (`deriveViewedPair`, `BookOutlineFollower.look`). So after DONE the follower re-derived with A OFF the page: something moved A up by ~900–1,000 pt (the walker's wheel-up estimate ≈990; the AX numbers — Red's live text area 22,250 pt tall with its bottom ~1,250 pt above the clip's top — put A's card ~850–950 pt above) after the landing had ended. The second bad landing (B, a block-home landing) says `content=0`: nothing changed during it either; its slide also came after.
2. **Nothing in the Outline restores the reading place once a landing has ended.** `LandingAuthority.contentChanged` returns at once when no landing is pending (LandingAuthority.swift:704); the Outline's only page-wide re-land is the WIDTH change (BookView.swift:2464); there is no scroll anchoring for a height change above the viewport (SwiftUI's `ScrollView` keeps the OFFSET, so content above that shrinks slides everything below it up). A height change above the target after DONE therefore moves the page's content under a fixed offset — exactly the captured state (the page near its maximum, scrollbar 0.996, notes 10–13 on the page).
3. **Two height changes above A that CAN land after a landing ends exist in the code**, and the bad state had a ~22,000-pt face above A in both landings (landing 1: The Red-Headed League's LIVE inline editor, carried from the Index; landing 2: Red-Headed's card just demoted from live to its read face, plus Scandal's unfolded read face):
   - (a) **the live inline editor's settle-net heal** (EditorView.swift:720–804): TEXTKIT2 §3's first-full-measure error (1–5 % off the settled layout; ~4.5 % of 22,250 pt ≈ 1,000 pt) heals only on a DISPLAY PASS, a storage edit or a width change — and an editor ~23,000 pt above the visible page gets no display pass, so its heal can come at any later moment;
   - (b) **a far expanded row's placeholder swap** (BookView.swift:3569–3583, 3646, 3716, 3776 — row 53 / 13u2b UNIT 1): an expanded row more than 2,500 pt from the viewport renders a placeholder at `outlineSurfaceHeight` (the remembered size, else an estimate), and its `StableSurface` reservation adopts the surface's real size when it settles — a remembered size that differs from the drawn height moves everything below it when the swap happens.
   - **The walker's lead, read right:** the signpost's `offset` is the manifest's sum of MEASURED/remembered heights above the target (`exact=1` both times — nothing above A was arithmetic), so 47,399 (19:14:33, Red's card holding its LIVE editor) vs 46,023 (19:19:44, the same row, Red no longer live) says Red's live card measured ~1,376 pt taller than what stood in its place five minutes later — the same order as the slide, and consistent with (a) or (b); it does not by itself say which, nor when.
4. **D-2's fix does not touch this class** (it changes only what a PENDING landing converges on); the walker's Terminal pass of the same sequence landed right (whether Terminal's metrics make the first measure exact is unknown).

**What is NOT established (and why no fix is written):** WHICH surface changed height, by how much, and when relative to DONE. Headless there is no road to it (no `xcodebuild test`, no app) — and a fix (e.g. scroll anchoring for a height change above the viewport while no landing is pending — Write's `keepUntouchedReadingLine` analogue, TEXTKIT2 §3's "a road that writes storage with no restore step keeps the reading line itself") would be a new Outline mechanism built on an unconfirmed trigger: the brief's "do NOT fix blind".

**The ticket (text for the orchestrator to file — Bug · Suspected · Books, suggest Medium; parent VX-677's record):** "Outline: a ~1,000-pt height change ABOVE the page after a landing has ended slides the landed note off the page (F-B2W-1). Seen twice in Study (walk vx677-b2, W-662-1) with a ~22,000-pt story expanded above the target (a live inline editor carried from the Index; a just-demoted read face). The landing itself settled (hops=0, top=0); the follower then re-derived note 10. Nothing re-lands or anchors after a landing ends. Candidates: the live editor's settle-net heal arriving late (an off-screen editor gets no display pass) · a far expanded row's placeholder/reservation swap at a remembered height ≠ its drawn height. Trace record: `notes/plans/fixes/vx-677-editor-panes-ai-door.md` DISPOSITIONS RECORD, D-3."

**What a stage trace needs (VX-668's precedent — a DEBUG trace in a held window, then the fix):** (1) a TEMPORARY DEBUG watch that keeps the Outline's "who grew" lines (`landingBox.rowHeights` diffs, today written only while a landing is pending — BookView.swift:1926–1932) for ~5 s AFTER the landing's DONE, plus each `NearViewportObserver` flip and each `StableSurface` adoption, and the inline editor's `remeasure`/`displayPassSettled` reports — each with a timestamp and the row's id; (2) the walk: Study, the Sherlock sample, Scandal's card unfolded as a read face (W-680-1's leftover), Red-Headed live in the Index at block 2, click Boscombe's row in the Index column, the mode tab Outline — then read the lines with `log show --last 5m --predicate 'process == "Variorum" AND eventMessage CONTAINS "BookOutline"'` (the walk's predicate `"landing surface=BookOutline"` matches only the shipping signpost, never the DEBUG story — which is why the story never surfaced). The first height change logged after DONE names the surface.

#### SELF-ASSESSMENT (dispositions — the first of the two reviews, the implementer's)

- **The traces held, each from evidence already in hand:** D-1 from the compiled binaries themselves (the merge is a fact of the machine code, not an inference from behaviour — the three products that ran it, and the rebuilt ones checked the same way); D-2 from the app's own landing stories in the window's logs (both trees) plus the code, with F-B3-1 excluded first by the read's shape, the binary and an independent number; D-3 only as far as the signposts, the captures and the code reach — the rest is said to be unknown, with what would know it.
- **What each verdict is worth today:** D-1's repair — the binary proves the pin now asserts what it says; its red/green are PREDICTED by mechanism (no honest run of the mounted Lenses cell exists yet in either window). D-2 — its mounted red-on-old is OBSERVED (twice, pre-fix); the fix's green is PREDICTED; the pure rule is green/red headless over the real source. D-3 — no verdict; a ticket.
- **Where I would want the cold reviewer to stare:**
  1. **D-2 changes VX-680's design in one respect** — a carried block that fits below the row no longer becomes the landing's home; the row does (with its drift corrected). I read this as the design's own words (P-680-2(a)'s "the row first", the batch-2 sequence, the guard's contract), not as a new product decision; a reviewer who reads P-680-2(a) as "the block's minimal reveal is the home whatever the row does" would call this a fork for her — the record's D-2 section states both readings' consequence (row 97 pt low in a small book vs at the top).
  2. **The act reads `box.page.tops[id]`, the report reads the report's own `top`.** They agree because the report writes `page.tops` before any act runs, and `homeIsTheRow` depends only on the block's place inside the row; an act reading a top one frame stale picks the same home unless the block's place inside the row moved in that frame (then the next look re-decides). The `?? false` arm (no top known yet — e.g. the LAND aim with the editor already laid out) keeps the as-built act.
  3. **D-1's detector is a lead, not a census** (capturing closures only); the MCP site it names is VX-692's to sweep, untouched here.
  4. **The removed DIAG** decided nothing; its removal is its own commit (`d1f72f488`).

#### VX-674 — as built (her ruling is the spec; member 5's line quotes it)

- **The face** (`Features/Tray/BlockHistoryPane.swift`, Mac-local): `CommitRowFace.restingWords(_:)` — nil for a NAMED commit (its name alone, read through the store's one rule `SavepointName.normalized`, so a blank name is no name), else `restingPreview(commit.text)`: leading whitespace and blank lines dropped, the FIRST LINE only (`Character.isNewline` — CRLF and U+2028 included), capped at `restingCap` = 160 `Character`s (never mid-grapheme; ≈ 4× what one line of the 420-pt column shows), trailing spaces trimmed, no ellipsis of the face's own (VX-144's lesson — the view's one-line tail truncation is the only one). Cost: the moment's first 160 characters plus its leading whitespace, from the row's already-loaded text — never a read, never the moment's length (P1).
- **The row** (`CommitRowView.restingLine`): when · the name, or else (unnamed) the words in `tokens.small` + `tokens.muted`, `lineLimit(1)`, tail truncation — her prose, so NOT case-folded by Terminal's chrome rule — · the ◦ (unchanged: `fixedSize`, faint, after the words), Restore at the end. The stamp and the glyph keep their widths; the words are what yields. A named commit's line is byte-identical to before.
- **Untouched:** the open row (its longer preview, details, Read in full, the name door), the read-in-full sheet, the phone's `SavepointRowFace` (its row already leads with the moment's words — **MOBILE PARITY: nothing mobile-visible**), every operation and query.
- **PROVISIONAL P-674-1 — an EMPTY unnamed moment at rest:** (a) BUILT: the open row's own words, "(empty take)" (`BlockPastCopy.emptyTake`), muted; (b) nothing — the stamp alone, as before the ruling.
- **Pins** (`VariorumTests/CommitRestingPreviewTests.swift`, 5; no closure inside a macro — VX-692): RED on `rev674` — `anUnnamedCommitRestsOnItsFirstWords` (deliberate, quiet, a blank name), `theRestingRowDrawsTheWordsMutedAfterTheStampAndOnlyWhenUnnamed` (all four voice sets: the words on the resting line, the second paragraph not, the muted ink present; the named row: its name, none of the words, no muted ink); GUARDS (green on both — the named anatomy, a new function's bound, the height): `aNamedCommitKeepsItsNameAlone`, `thePreviewIsTheFirstLineBoundedAndNeverCutsAGrapheme` (leading blanks, CRLF, trailing spaces, the cap, a seven-scalar grapheme + a decomposed é run cut on a whole é, no face ellipsis, the empty words), `aLongFirstLineStillRestsOnOneLine` (≤ 34 pt, both voices, prints the height). **Justified edit:** `CommitsPageLedgerTests.theRestingLineSaysWhenTheNameAndTheQuietGlyphOnly`'s last half held "no words at rest" on an UNNAMED commit — the ruling reverses exactly that, so the half now holds it for a NAMED commit (same words, named); the open-row half unchanged. `theRowIsCompactAndItsActsAreWords` is untouched and now measures an unnamed row WITH its preview against the same ≤ 34-pt bound.

Every chord the helper shows is now READ from the act a row names; a re-picked key moves in the registry alone.

**PROVISIONAL P-572-1 — the grouping:** (a) BUILT: her nine headings exactly as they shipped (Writing · Formatting · Structure · Takes · Find in a note · Navigation · Windows & panes · The note's bars · MCP), the same rows under each in the same order, re-ordered as the writing day (writing first, the AI door last — the shipped order led with Takes and put MCP third), each with ONE muted line of what it holds ("commit, versions, undo, comments, callouts" · "bold, italic, headings, lists, links" · "divide, extract, merge, a new block" · "a block's takes — new, choose, step, archive" · "find, replace, the next match" · "modes, find anything, new notes, wings" · "the Open list, back and forward, splits, the quick note" · "the bottom bar's menus and the masthead's tags" · "an AI client's asks, shares and changes"), so a collapsed list still says where to look; (b) the shipped order, no lines; (c) regroup by surface (Write · Takes · Books · Windows · the AI door), renaming headings.

**PROVISIONAL P-572-2 — the face** (`Features/Help/ShortcutsTab.swift`; the helper window's default 680×560 → 760×620, the minimum 640×480 kept): a search field pinned above the list (the voiced field's idiom, the caret in it on arrival, ⎋ or its ✕ clears) · "Every shortcut here is also a menu item — the menus are the map." kept under it · each heading ONE press for the whole line (▸ folded / ▾ open), its title in the voice's heading face (Terminal lowercases it as chrome), its one line muted and truncating, its count faint at the end ("13"; "1 of 12" while searching) · rows indented under the title: the chord column a FIXED 190 pt (the widest token, "Format ▸ Strikethrough" / "View ▸ Close All Notes", measures 176.8 pt in Terminal's 13-pt mono and 170.0 in Study's 12.5 — measured headless with AppKit over both fonts), each chord ONE text, `lineLimit(1)` + `fixedSize()`, in the voice's mono and ACCENT ink, two chords "a / b" with a faint slash; the description in the voice's OWN face (`tokens.body` — Iowan Old Style 16 in Study, the 14-pt mono in Terminal), ink, its wrapped lines hanging in their own column (`lineLimit(12)`, pinned never to cut) · 7 pt above and below each row and a voice hairline (`tokens.line`) between rows and between groups — the separation that keeps a long description from reading as the next row · the empty search: "No shortcut matches “…” — try a word for what it does, or keys like cmd shift d." Options: (a) as built; (b) the chord column in ink with the descriptions muted (the old register); (c) no hairlines — spacing alone.

**PROVISIONAL P-572-3 — searching a chord by name:** (a) BUILT: a query made only of modifier words or glyphs (cmd/command ⌘ · shift ⇧ · opt/option/alt ⌥ · ctrl/control ⌃, "+" or spaces between) and at most ONE key (a character, or return/enter ⏎ · esc/escape ⎋ · tab · up/down/left/right · comma · slash) matches the chords with EXACTLY those modifiers and that key ("cmd shift d" is ⌘⇧D, never ⌥⌘⇧D; a label's modifier order never matters; a digit falls in a span — "cmd shift 3" is ⌘⇧1–9), modifiers alone list every chord carrying them; anything else is a word search; (b) glyphs only (pasted); (c) words only — no chord reading.

**PROVISIONAL P-572-4 — Settings ▸ Shortcuts:** (a) BUILT: the pane keeps its own card face over the SAME catalog (the Settings window already has its one search, which finds every row); (b) the pane renders the helper's folded face too (without a second search field).

**The filter** (`ShortcutCatalogFilter`, pure): at rest every heading shows and a group shows its rows only when SHE opened it; a word search ANDs case- and accent-blind terms over a row's chords, words, heading and the VERBS of the acts it names (the registry's names — "callout" finds ⌥⌘C), shows the matching rows in their groups with every such group OPEN and hides the rest; a heading folded while searching folds for that query only (`ShortcutsTabModel.searchCollapsed`, emptied by every new query) — her own folding is never touched by a search, and clearing the query returns exactly what she had opened.

**Rails:** P-1 (the rows, headings and chords from the registry; the view-local controls — the field, the folds — are undeclared, the Library `sort ▾` precedent: they change what she sees, never the store); P-8 (tokens only — the literal-colour pin's scan covers the new files; every bare `fixedSize()` carries `lineLimit(1)` within its four lines — `ChromeLineLimitTests`); no operation, query, schema, `Sync/` or editor-seam line touched. **MOBILE PARITY: nothing mobile-visible** (the helper is Mac-only; the phone has no shortcuts pane).

#### VX-668 — the inspector pin (PART 1 step 1 — WRITTEN AND COMPILED; its run is the window's)

**The one structural difference, built:** the VX-440 pin mounts the pane BESIDE the detail in an `HStack` with a model the TEST made, and dismisses the sheet by writing `history.reading = nil`. The app builds the pane INSIDE the detail's inspector column (`PostDetailView`'s `SideColumn` panel, case `.blockHistory` — the pane constructed with NO model, so its model is the pane's own `@State`), reached by Write's own history door, and the sheet goes by its Restore button's own `dismiss()`. The new suite `RestoreLandingInspectorTests` (`VariorumTests/RestoreLandingInspectorTests.swift`, 3 tests) is that structure and nothing else changed — the staging every test shares (`stage`): the REAL detail alone in a 1520×780 window, Write arrived; a moment committed on block 30 of 40 ("first light"); her caret at the block's END and words TYPED there through the editor's own road (`insertText` — the coordinator dirty, the walk's "Write typed a third ending, to be undone by the sheet"); Write's OWN history door (`EditorViewModel.requestBlockHistory` — the caret's block, the focused take) → the router → the detail's `showBlockHistory` → the inspector stands the pane (premises `#require`d, session-scoped so a parallel suite's detail cannot answer: the pane's model published for THIS note on her block, Write's own fallback sheet `blockHistoryRequest` never armed — the router took the request — and no sheet on the window; the moment listed; `PostDetailOracle.inspectorPanel` printed, not required).
- `aSheetRestoreFromTheInspectorLandsAtTheBlocksStart` — **THE PIN.** The row's Read-in-full door (`readInFull`, its body) on the INSPECTOR's model → the REAL sheet presented from the inspector's pane (`window.attachedSheet`) → its Restore pressed AS ITS BUTTON PRESSES IT (`CommitReadingSheet.pressRestore`: `onRestore(false)`, then the sheet's OWN `dismiss()`) → within 5 s Write landed (`restoreLandings` rose) with the caret at the restored block's START; the W-2 order (`[.dismissed, .acted]`) and the store's restored words as fixture lines. **RED = VX-668 reproduced in-host** (then it is the fix's red-on-old pin, and the run's own `VX668-TRACE` lines name the failed link in-host); **GREEN = the host still does not reproduce** — the walk's trace (B5-2) decides.
- `aRowRestoreFromTheInspectorLandsAtTheBlocksStart` — **THE CONTROL**, the walk's own: the same staging, the pane row's Restore (`model.restore`, `CommitRowView`'s `onRestore` body). GREEN on every tree; RED would mean this harness cannot land from the inspector at all, and the pin's verdict would be NO verdict.
- `aSheetRestoreFromTheInspectorInTheShellLandsAtTheBlocksStart` — the pin again with the WHOLE shell around the detail (`RootView`, `services: nil` — the pane mount rule, the detail's landing-generation `.id`, the shell's chrome; the PaneSplitTests rig: the Bookcase first, then `pendingSelection = .post`). A second shape for the same window, because a run of either is all this session cannot get: red here and green above would say the miss lives in the shell's composition.

VX-692 (F-B3-1): no closure-bearing expression inside `#expect`/`#require` — every awaited or closure read bound to a local first; the binary detector agrees.

**The DEBUG pin doors it needs** (`Features/Tray/BlockHistoryPane.swift`, DEBUG-only, decides nothing — these STAY with the pin, unlike the trace): `BlockHistoryPaneOracle` (the 18k C4 owner shape over `OwnedRegistrations`) — the pane publishes the model it holds (`.onAppear`, released `.onDisappear`; keyed by note), and the read-in-full sheet publishes its Restore button's own action (keyed by commit). The button's action body moved into `CommitReadingSheet.pressRestore()` — byte-identical behaviour (`if predatesSplit { confirmingRestore = true } else { onRestore(false); dismiss() }`), so the door IS what the button does. Why a door at all: in-process SwiftUI builds no accessibility nodes (`ComposedSliders`' finding) and a synthetic click needs a frame probe the sheet does not have; the published closure is the button's own action, captured from the mounted sheet, exactly as the button's closure is.

#### VX668-TRACE — as landed (PART 1 step 2, the declared trace; TEMPORARY — REMOVED BEFORE MERGE)

**Why it lands beside the pin rather than after it:** step 2 is conditioned on the pin failing to reproduce, and this session cannot run the pin — so the trace rides the same window. It costs nothing if the pin is red (its run's own log then carries the lines in-host — "instrument the pin's run freely"), and it is the only road to the failed link if the pin is green. **LOG LINES ONLY:** every site writes one `NSLog` line, prefix `VX668-TRACE t=<uptime s>`, and changes nothing a product path reads; the one non-log state is the trace's own clock (`VX668Trace.lastMark` — the noisy points log only within 4 s of a MARK, so an ordinary DEBUG run is not flooded) and a DEBUG-only road word on the model (`vx668Road`, read only by the log). Every site is `#if DEBUG`-gated and marked `// VX668-TRACE`; the helper file `Features/Tray/VX668Trace.swift` is DEBUG end to end. Two NON-DEBUG syntax touches, both inert and both removed with the trace: `EditorView`'s `onChange` closure names its two parameters (`old, new` for `_, _`), and `CommandRouter.pendingRestoreLanding` carries a `didSet` whose body is DEBUG-only (an observer on an `@Observable` property — the house's own precedent, `LibraryCatalogModel.noteHits`). **Removal = reverse-apply the trace commit's App half, from the repo root:** `git show f97aa5e79 -- App/Variorum/Variorum | git apply -R` (dry-run `--check` CLEAN at the tip `f97aa5e79`; a later edit to one of those lines turns it into a hand removal — grep `VX668-TRACE` / `VX668Trace`, 6 files). Never `git revert` the whole commit: it carries this record's own sections too.

**The points (both roads — the road word rides each restore line):**
- **(a) the parcel's post** — `BlockHistoryModel.restore`: `restore-begin road=sheet|row …` (MARK), `restore-flushed`, `post road=… parcel=<id8> post=… block=… take=… textLen=… routerDetailPost=… routerMode=…` (MARK), or `restore-refused`. The sheet road around it: `sheet-foot act=…` (MARK), `sheet-onDismiss` (SwiftUI's own call, MARK), `sheet-dismissed pending=… parcelOnRouter=…` (the Task it starts).
- **EVERY write of the parcel** — `parcel-set old=<id8|nil> new=<id8|nil> by=<the writer's four frames>` (`CommandRouter.pendingRestoreLanding`'s `didSet`): the post, each consumer's take, the router's clears — and any writer nobody enumerated.
- **(b) the push** — `EditorView.onChange`: `onChange editor=<model address> post=… inline=… old=… new=… key=… main=… sheet=… appKey=…`; and every `EditorView` BODY evaluation inside the window: `editor-body editor=… parcel=<what the body READ> …` — the line that says whether the body re-ran at all after the post, and what value the gate saw. The consumer: `consume editor=… scroll=… parcel=… parcelPost=… caret=…` then `consume-took` (MARK).
- **(c) the settle hop** — `overlayLayoutDidSettle`'s hop: `settle-hop editor=… gen=… parked=… routerParcel=… dirty=… caret=… <window state>`; the landing's outcome every time it is asked: `land … outcome=nothing-parked|dropped (expired/dirty)|absent|not-yet (shown/textView/scroll)|landed start=… caret=…`; the delivery around the rewrite: `projection editor=… decision=… delivery=… dirty=… caret=…` and `projection-done … caret=…` (the caret the rewrite left).
- **Every other road that can take or clear it, and the lifecycles a remount would show:** `takes-consume` / `takes-consume-took` (the Takes/Lenses boards); `router-clear why=register-other|unregister`; `register-detail` / `unregister-detail` (a detail re-registering); `editor-start` / `editor-teardown` (a new editor address = a remount).

**The reading key (what each candidate looks like in the lines — the record's step 6 named two, the rest are the ones its elimination leaves standing):**

| Candidate | Its signature after `post road=sheet` |
|---|---|
| S1 — the body never re-ran (the observation not re-registered across the sheet's teardown) | NO `editor-body` line reading the parcel, NO `onChange` — until the window closes |
| S2 — the parcel changed and was cleared inside one update (the gate saw no change) | a `parcel-set … new=nil by=<writer>` BEFORE the next `editor-body … parcel=nil`, and no `onChange` |
| S3 — the push fired, the consumer declined | `onChange … new=<id>` then `consume … scroll=false` (or `parcelPost` ≠ the editor's post), no `consume-took` |
| S4 — consumed, then dropped | `consume-took` then `land … outcome=dropped dirty=true` (or `expired=true`) / `absent` / `not-yet` with no later `settle-hop` |
| S5 — a remount after the post | `editor-teardown` + `editor-start` (a NEW editor address), or `unregister-detail` + `register-detail`, between the post and any `onChange` — the new editor's first body reads the parcel as its INITIAL value, so its `onChange` never fires |
| S6 — landed, then undone | `land … outcome=landed start=S` then a later `projection-done … caret=<the block's end>` |

**The trace, the two roads side by side** (`t=` uptime s; full lines in the logs):
- Sheet: `sheet-foot act=restore` .779 → `sheet-onDismiss` .783 → (270 ms) `sheet-dismissed pending=true parcelOnRouter=nil` / `restore-begin road=sheet` / `restore-flushed` 871.053 → `projection … decision=refetchFresh delivery=true … caret=121` .069 → **`parcel-set new=58D77967` / `post road=sheet … textLen=38 routerMode=write` .091** → `editor-body parcel=58D77967` → `onChange old=nil new=58D77967` → **`consume … scroll=true parcel=58D77967 parcelPost=C6B3C064 caret=121`** → `parcel-set old=58D77967 new=nil` / `consume-took` → **`land … outcome=not-yet shown=false textView=true scroll=true … caret=121`** .112 → `projection … decision=differential delivery=false … caret=121` .119 → `projection-done … decision=differential caret=66` .120 → `editor-body parcel=nil` → **nothing more: no `settle-hop` line, no later `land` line** (the 4-s window was open).
- Row: `restore-begin road=row` / `restore-flushed` 918.228 → `projection … refetchFresh … caret=121` .250 → `projection … differential … caret=121` .251 → `projection-done … differential caret=66` .252 → **`post road=row parcel=D1801DC4`** .265 → `editor-body parcel=D1801DC4` → `onChange` → **`consume … caret=66`** → `consume-took` → **`land … outcome=landed start=28 caret=28`** .282.

**The first line that differs after `post road=…`:** `consume … caret=121` (sheet) vs `consume … caret=66` (row) — on the sheet road the restored words have NOT reached the editor when the parcel is consumed; then `land … outcome=not-yet shown=false` vs `land … outcome=landed start=28`. **Reading key: S4** ("consumed … `not-yet` with no later `settle-hop`"). What the lines establish: on the sheet road the parcel is posted BETWEEN the restore's two projection deliveries (`refetchFresh` .069 → post .091 → `differential` rewrite .119), while on the row road both deliveries land before the post (.250/.251/.252 → post .265); the sheet road's landing therefore asks before the words are shown, parks `not-yet`, and is never asked again — the differential rewrite leaves the caret at 66 (block 2's end) and no `settle-hop` (the only re-ask road the trace has) fires in the window. No S1/S2/S3/S5/S6 signature: the body re-ran and read the parcel, `onChange` fired, the consumer took it, the editor address `0x…7691b8f100` never changed (no remount), no `register`/`unregister-detail`, no landed-then-undone. Not traced beyond the lines (why the differential rewrite schedules no settle hop is the implementer's). **Note for the logs:** `log show --predicate 'eventMessage CONTAINS "VX668-TRACE"'` returned ZERO trace lines (`trace-sheet-study.logshow-empty.txt`) because the launch redirected stderr: the NSLog lines went to `app-stderr.log` (22 lines at T1's end); `trace-sheet-study.log` (21 lines) and `trace-row-study.log` (18 lines) are extracted from it.

#### Findings (walker-observed; for the orchestrator)

- **F-FW-1 (VX-668 — the trace's answer, above):** S4 on the sheet road: the parcel posted between the restore's `refetchFresh` and `differential` deliveries, consumed `not-yet shown=false`, never re-asked (no `settle-hop` after the rewrite). #### F-1 — VX-668: THE WHY (answered from the code, the walk's raw lines and two offscreen probes)

**The question:** after the sheet road's differential rewrite (`projection-done … decision=differential caret=66`, t…120) no `settle-hop` line came in the 4-s window, so the parked landing was never asked again. What distinguishes this rewrite from the ones batch 5's in-host trace shows followed by a hop?

**The answer: the settle hop is armed ONLY by a display pass of the text view ITSELF, and on macOS 27 a TextKit 2 storage edit that leaves the text view's frame where it was never makes one.**
1. **The one trigger.** `EditorViewModel.overlayLayoutDidSettle` (the N14 hop that calls `landRestoreIfShown`) is scheduled from `EditorTextView.viewWillDraw` → `onLayoutSettle` (`EditorViewModel.attach`, :497) and from two direct callers that are not this road (the width reflow, the re-font keeper). The rewrite's storage edit bumps `overlayGeometryGeneration` (`storageDidEdit`, :1342, suppressed writes included) — but a bump schedules nothing by itself; it waits for the text view's next `viewWillDraw`.
2. **TK2 draws its text in its OWN subviews, not in the text view.** Measured (offscreen probe on a bare TK2 `NSTextView`, this machine's macOS 27.0.1; sources + logs in the gitignored `App/Variorum/DerivedData/vx677-c/logs/probe/`): the text view holds `_NSTextSelectionView`, `_NSTextRenderingSurfacesGroupView` and `_NSTextContentView` → one `_NSTextViewportElementView` per laid-out fragment. After a programmatic `replaceCharacters` that removes the walk's typed ending (the restore's shape) the text view's `needsDisplay` stays false and its `viewWillDraw` runs **0 times** over five display passes; the same for an `insertText` keystroke; an edit that GROWS the document (its frame changes) runs it **2 times** (`probe1-A.log`, never ordered in; `probe1-B.log`, ordered in off-display — the same numbers). Setting `needsDisplay = true` on the text view after the same edit makes the app's own run-loop display cycle run its `viewWillDraw` (`probe2.log`: 1 → 2, no explicit `displayIfNeeded`).
3. **The walk's note is exactly the frame-constant case.** Two short blocks in a 1240×840 window: the page's frame is FLOORED (`EditorTextView.lastLineBottomForRoom`'s floored branch, read from `usageBoundsForTextContainer` — a HIGH-WATER MARK while the document holds text, TEXTKIT2 §3's 17o finding), so a restore that REMOVES words can never move the frame. No display pass of the text view → no `viewWillDraw` → no hop → the landing parked `not-yet` at consume is never re-asked (S4 of the reading key, by the mechanism above).
4. **Why the in-host trace DID hop on the same interleaving** (`DerivedData/vx677-final/logs/R13-b5verdict.log`: `refetchFresh` .508 → `post` .530 → `consume` → `land … not-yet` .551 → `differential` .587 → **`settle-hop gen=61` .601 → `land … landed`** — the walk's own order, on BOTH in-host roads): `RestoreLandingInspectorTests` restores block 30 of a 40-paragraph note, the TALL case, whose frame is AppKit's proposed content height — the rewrite that removes a wrapped line of typing changes it, so the text view itself displays and the hop comes. The in-host pin was green because its note is long, not because its order differed.
5. **Why the row road landed live:** its post fell AFTER both deliveries (`differential` .251 → `post` .265), so the consume-time ask found the words already shown and landed at once — the hop was never needed (and never came: no `settle-hop` line on that road either). Which of the two lands first — the restore's own continuation after `runner.run` (the post) or the editor's fresh-read Task (the rewrite) — is a race on either road; nothing orders it. The sheet road lost it live; the row road can lose it too.

**So the defect is not the push, not a remount, and not the sheet:** it is that the landing's ONE re-ask rides a display pass TextKit 2 does not make for a rewrite that keeps the text view's frame. The record's standing shape (pull the parcel at the settle hop) would not have helped — no hop fires. **The fix the WHY demands:** the rewrite a parked landing waits for makes that display pass CERTAIN — `adoptRewrite` (the one tiling-write door every executor passes after writing the storage) sets the text view's `needsDisplay` while a landing is parked, so the hop runs after the pass that laid the rewritten words out and the landing lands there, exactly as its design says ("spent at the settle hop after that rewrite"). The house's own precedent for exactly this certainty: VX-282 R1-2's `settleMarkdownCaret` ("The pass is made CERTAIN here (`needsDisplay`)… Deliberately not a direct `overlayLayoutDidSettle()`… a direct hop can run BEFORE the layout pass"). The row road (consume after the rewrite) is unchanged — it lands at consume, nothing is parked, nothing is armed.

**As built.** The PIN (`VariorumTests/RestoreLandingRewriteHopTests.swift`, suite `RestoreLandingRewriteHopTests`, committed on the PRE-FIX tree `4088f758b`): the walk's own note shape (two short blocks, the restored block last, Study, the walk's 1240×840 window), the moment committed, her typing at the block's END, flushed (the restore's own first step); then Write's REAL push takes the parcel (posted exactly as `BlockHistoryModel.restore` posts it) while the page still shows her typing — `#require`d — and only then the restore's store write through the pane's own road (`session.runner.run { $0.restoreCommit(…) }`); the rewrite lands and the text view's frame is `#require`d UNCHANGED; then within 4 s Write must land at the block's START. RED on the pre-fix tree by the mechanism above (no hop, never landed, the caret at the END); GREEN on the fix. The GUARD `aLandingWhoseWordsAreAlreadyShownLandsAtOnce` is the row road's order on the same note (the rewrite shown first, the parcel after → lands at consume) — green on both. The FIX (`8020725d6`): `EditorViewModel.adoptRewrite` — `if parkedRestoreLanding != nil { textView?.needsDisplay = true }`, with its comment; nothing else. `RestoreLandingInspectorTests` (3) stays the green guard (and is predicted green on `rev668` too — the WHY's own prediction, C1). **MOBILE PARITY: nothing mobile-visible.**

**The wider class, recorded — NOT this unit's (finding F-FX-1 below):** every other consumer of the N14 hop after a frame-constant edit (the badges' and decorations' post-layout refresh, R1-1's deferred indicator refresh, `considerLongBlockLayout`) shares the same missing trigger on macOS 27; their symptom would be overlay geometry left at pre-edit frames until something else displays the text view. Not walked, not pinned, not touched here.

#### F-2 — DA-1: the quiet-line pin (`MCPShowOfferFacesTests.afterTheToastFadesTheAskHoldsTheQuietLine`, red on post in R7 — `line?.text → nil` after the fade)

**VX-692's macro shape checked FIRST — RULED OUT.** The test body holds no closure inside `#expect`/`#require` (its one closure, `eventually { session.status.current == nil }`, is a plain argument), and the binary detector over `vx677-b5/post`'s test bundle (`nm … | grep -E 'fU[0-9]*_TA\.[0-9]+$'`) finds only F-D-1's MCP lead (`MCPShareFacesTests.theIndicatorSaysOnlyWhatIsTrue`) and four Foundation thunks — nothing in this suite.

**The mechanism (traced in the code; the timing agrees):** `Rig.init` → `MCPDoor.attach(session:windows:)` → `observe(_:)` starts the door's store observation; its FIRST snapshot is delivered asynchronously, a few milliseconds later. The pin's in-memory store has the server switch OFF (never set), so that delivery runs `reconcileServer()` → `guard snapshot.enabled else { stopServer(ending: .serverOff) … }` → `stopServer` → `adoptConnected([])` → `if let offer = showOffer, !names.contains(offer.request.clientName) { endShowOffer() }` — the ask ENDS ("its client is gone", ruling (2)), and `lineItem` has nothing to show. The pin reads the line after the 60-ms fade plus a 300-ms wait, i.e. after that delivery (its failure at 0.367 s is that sum); every other offer pin reads synchronously after `agentShow` returns — before the delivery can run (it needs an await) — and passes. R6's extra red on `rev675` (13/2) is the same test, the same mechanism, independent of the revert.

**So it is a harness fact, not a defect:** an ask can only arrive over a live connection, which needs the server ON and the client connected; the pin staged an ask from a client that was never connected on a store whose server was OFF, and the door did exactly what it must when it heard that (a server that stops disconnects everyone, so every ask ends). Live (the final walk's W-676-1, both voices) the server was on and claude-code connected, and the line held to +21.5 / +25.5 s. **No production change.**

**The repair (red on `rev676`, green on `post` — both by mechanism; runs PENDING claim C):** the store's server switch ON before the rig attaches (`setMCPSwitch(.server, on: true)` — the door's `reconcileServer` then waits for a library, `serverAllowed: false`, and never stops anything); a `#require`d premise that the door adopted that first snapshot (`snapshot.enabled`, bound to a local first — VX-692); `previewConnected(["claude-code"])` — the asking client connected, as the host reports it (the seam `theAskEndsWhenItsClientDisconnects` already uses); then the ask, the fade, the 300-ms wait and the same three expectations, plus one non-vacuity line (`showOffer != nil` — nothing ended it). On `rev676` the old etiquette refuses the away ask (`verdict == .refuse`), no ask stands, the line reads nil → RED on its first and line expectations; on `post` the ask stands through the fade → GREEN. Nothing else in the suite moved.


#### F-3 — F-FW-2: the helper window's missing theme (VX-572's own scope)

**The trace (read, confirmed):** `VariorumApp.swift`'s `Window("Shortcuts & Dictionary")` built `HelperView()` bare. `HelperView` reads `@Environment(ThemeStore.self) private var themeStore: ThemeStore?` and resolves `themeStore?.tokens(for: scheme) ?? .fallback(for: scheme)`; `VoiceTokens.fallback` is Terminal. Every OTHER window root owns its theme: `RootView` (`_theme = State(initialValue: ThemeStore(session:))`, `.environment(theme)`, `.task { await theme.start() }`), `NoteWindowView`, `QuickNoteView` and `AppSettingsView` the same — the helper's scene was the one root with none, so it drew Terminal under any voice (the walk's S-572-1/2). Batch 4's `ShortcutsTabFaceTests` hands the TAB its tokens itself, so it could not see a missing environment (C7 below proves it: all five stay green on the revert).

**The fix:** the window's content moved out of the scene closure into `Features/Help/HelperSceneContent.swift` (`SettingsSceneContent`'s VX-224 precedent — a pin mounts exactly what the window shows), and `VariorumApp.swift`'s ONE region changed (the helper `Window`'s closure, now `HelperSceneContent(state: bootstrap.state)`; VX-678's `appDelegate.services = fresh` ~:255 untouched). Its ready arm is `HelperThemedRoot` — the other roots' exact shape: a `ThemeStore` of the window's own over the open session, `.environment(theme)`, `.task { await theme.start() }`, and `.id(ObjectIdentifier(session))` (a new library is a fresh store). Every other state keeps the bare `HelperView()` and its fallback (no open library, no voice to read). The Dictionary tab's system face is F-B4-3, HER call — not widened. **MOBILE PARITY: nothing mobile-visible** (the helper is Mac-only).

**The pin door** (DEBUG only, decides nothing — `BlockHistoryPaneOracle`'s kind): `HelperViewOracle` — the root registers the theme it gave its window, keyed by the session (`onAppear` / `onDisappear`), and `HelperView`'s body publishes the tokens it drew in, keyed by the theme that reached it (or apart, as `lastUnthemed`). In-process SwiftUI builds no accessibility tree to read a face from (`SurfaceComposition`'s recorded limit), so the pin asks the door "under THIS library, what did the window draw?".

**Pins** (`VariorumTests/HelperWindowThemeTests.swift`, suite `HelperWindowThemeTests`, 3): THE PIN `theHelperWindowDrawsItsDescriptionsInTheLibrarysVoice` — `HelperSceneContent(state: .ready(session))` mounted in a 760×620 window over an in-memory store set to Study; within 5 s the helper drew in Study, its description face is Study's body face (`VoiceTokens.studyLight.body`, not the mono), and `ShortcutRowView` composes it — RED on the seam commit `a01429c1b` (no theme reaches the helper) and on `vx677-c/revFW2`, GREEN on the fix; GUARDS (green on both): `aHelperWithNoOpenLibraryKeepsItsFallbackFace` (`.opening` → the Terminal fallback) and the source guard `theAppsHelperWindowHostsItsSceneContent` (the scene's closure is `HelperSceneContent(state: bootstrap.state)`, never a bare `HelperView()` — the mounted pin's "as the app does" premise, held as source).

#### SELF-ASSESSMENT (the fixes unit — the first of the two reviews, the implementer's)

- **The WHY, not a guess.** The trace said WHERE (S4: consumed `not-yet`, never re-asked); the code said WHAT re-asks (only the N14 hop, only from the text view's own `viewWillDraw`); two offscreen probes on bare TK2 said WHY none came (a frame-constant storage edit never displays the `NSTextView` itself on macOS 27.0.1 — TK2 draws in `_NSTextViewportElementView` subviews) and what makes it come (`needsDisplay`); and the in-host R13 log, read raw, showed the same interleaving landing ONLY because its long note's rewrite moved the frame. The record's standing fix shape (pull at the hop) would not have worked — there was no hop.
- **What each pin is worth today:** every pin is compiled into every product, binary-checked (the VX-692 detector finds only F-D-1's lead; each revert product differs from `post` only in its own function); none is run — every red/green is PREDICTED by mechanism and owed to claim C.
- **Where I would want the cold reviewer to stare:**
  1. **The fix's trigger is `parkedRestoreLanding != nil` at `adoptRewrite`** — every executor's door (differential, full render, the structural rewrites). A rewrite that is NOT the one carrying the restored words (e.g. a structural act she makes inside the 2-s patience) also arms the display; the hop then asks the landing, which lands only if the slot shows the words and the buffer is clean — the landing's own guards, unchanged. One extra display pass of the text view's own layer while a landing waits; nothing otherwise.
  2. **`needsDisplay` (not a direct `overlayLayoutDidSettle()`)** — VX-282 R1-2's stated reason: a direct hop can run before the pass that lays the rewrite out and spend the generation on pre-layout frames.
  3. **THE PIN stages the post BEFORE the restore's write** (the walk's post fell between the two deliveries). Argued in its header: `refetchFresh` touches neither the page nor the landing, so the editor holds the walk's state when the rewrite lands, and the order can no longer flip; both premises (`taken before the rewrite`, `the frame unchanged`) are `#require`d so a drift fails as a fixture, never as a hollow green.
  4. **DA-1's repair changes staging, not assertions** — the store's server ON + `previewConnected(["claude-code"])`; the three original expectations are byte-identical. The one new line (`showOffer != nil`) is non-vacuity.
  5. **F-FW-2's root starts a FIFTH `ThemeStore` on the session** (one per window root, as the four others) — each `start()` adopts the same typography and receipt style from the same store (idempotent); `.id(ObjectIdentifier(session))` re-makes it for a new library.
  6. **One DEBUG pin door was added** (`HelperViewOracle`, decides nothing; two publish sites — the root's `onAppear`/`onDisappear` and `HelperView`'s body) — and batch 5's DEBUG doors stay.
- **Rails kept:** zero lines under `Sources/` (incl. `Sync/`), `App/Variorum/Shared/` or `VariorumMobile/`; the editor seam's machinery untouched beyond the one `needsDisplay` line — no TK1 API, nothing written to the live storage, the selection or `BlockRangeIndex`, `runStructural` untouched, attribute stamps not involved; the overlap file `VariorumApp.swift` touched in ONE region (the helper `Window`'s closure), VX-678's services line untouched; no copy change, no new dependency, no schema, no operation. **MOBILE PARITY: nothing mobile-visible** (all Mac-only files).

#### FINDINGS RECORDED, NOT FIXED (the fixes unit — for the orchestrator to ticket or dismiss)

- **F-FX-1 (Suspected · Editor, suggest Medium — the WHY's wider class):** every OTHER consumer of the N14 settle hop meets the same missing trigger after a frame-constant edit on macOS 27 — `refreshBadges`/`refreshDecorations`' post-layout refresh after typing or a take switch in a short (floored) note, R1-1's deferred indicator refresh, `considerLongBlockLayout`. Their symptom would be overlay geometry left at pre-edit frames until something else displays the text view itself. Not walked, not pinned, not touched (a second unit).
- **F-FX-2 (TEXTKIT2_NOTES candidate — canon is the closeout's):** "macOS 27.0.1: a TK2 `NSTextView` renders its text in its own subviews (`_NSTextContentView` → one `_NSTextViewportElementView` per laid-out fragment, beside `_NSTextSelectionView` and `_NSTextRenderingSurfacesGroupView`); a storage edit that leaves the text view's frame unchanged — a programmatic replace or `insertText` — never runs the text view's own `viewWillDraw` and never sets its `needsDisplay` (0 calls over five display passes; a frame-growing edit: 2); setting `needsDisplay = true` makes the next run-loop display cycle run it. **The rule:** a consumer of the text view's own display pass must make that pass certain after an edit it waits on." (Probes and logs: `App/Variorum/DerivedData/vx677-c/logs/probe/`.)
- **F-FX-3 (Test-only, Low):** `MCPShowOfferFacesTests`' other pins read the seam synchronously after `agentShow`, before the door's first snapshot — safe by structure today; any future pin that AWAITS after an ask on a server-off store meets DA-1's end. The repaired pin's staging (server on + the client connected) is the shape to copy.

### SurfaceContracts — the contract lines DRAFTED for the merge (batch 2's carry family + VX-668's sheet landing; the ladder's F-L5 answered; the orchestrator lands them at closeout — nothing in canon is edited here)

**§The Bookcase and books, the mode-row paragraph** — the VX-630 carry sentence grows the family's facts IN PLACE. Replace: "Outline → Index carries the live block at the caret, and Index → Outline carries it back, the Outline arriving with the note live at her block and offset (VX-630, PROVISIONAL P-B4-1); the departing surface never eats the arriving one's reveal." with: "Outline → Index carries the live block at the caret, and Index → Outline carries it back block-precise: the Outline arrives with the note live at her block and offset, and the carried block itself is brought onto the page inside the arrival's one landing — the note's row first, and the block's own minimal reveal only when it would not fit below the row's top (a block below the fold rests at the page's foot; a block taller than the page shows her caret's line) (VX-630; VX-680, P-680-1/2); the departing surface never eats the arriving one's reveal. Arrivals into Read ▸ Pages open at the carried note's page, never page one (VX-663); leaving Read ▸ Scroll carries the story on screen, not the entered or last-focused note (VX-681); the Index's carry anchor follows carried arrivals too, so a later Outline carry lands the note on screen, never a stale one (VX-662); and on every landing surface, a landing whose page cannot move far enough to close its target is home at the page's floor and settles at the hold, instead of correcting until its budget gives up (VX-624)."

## Rulings log

*(anything needing her lands here PROVISIONAL-and-continue, bundled for the closeout checklist; anything gating the core STOPS)*

**The architect's rulings on batch 1's design fork (2026-10-28, received by the implementer 14:30 MDT; pasted VERBATIM):**

**A-677-1 (the VX-664 mechanism — shape B APPROVED).** The ring-end signal is the store's own delivery: ONE pure rule in PaneTextAuthority, consulted at each ring owner's existing settle(isPending:) seam with the live take's outgoing and incoming stored text — an outside write landed iff a pane is live, nothing of hers is pending for that take, and the incoming stored text differs from what the pane holds; the owner then runs VX-222/VX-440's ring-end (blur, provably-empty flush, the pane goes read and adopts through the existing read arm). Grounds: complete BY CONSTRUCTION over every present and future road (anything changing stored text reaches the owner through the observation → settle seam), stored state read at wire time (the overlay/sink law) rather than writers remembering to post (shape A's enumeration fragility — P-4's hazard, the D3/D4 audit class), zero Sync/, the MCP door byte-unchanged, and P-3 whole: the authority stays the one liveness brain and a live pane never adopts — it stops being live. For the record: shape A would NOT be preferable even in a future sync round — her ruling keys on "an outside write landed on the live pane's OWN take", never on who wrote it, so attribution buys nothing and nothing here waits for sync. The pending-guard is correct per her ruling: the covered roads meet an empty pending by the flush-first rule, and the un-flushable race is the ticketed residual below. Your sweep table must still verdict EVERY road (including your unenumerated finds like the board's Split at Newlines) as COVERED-by-the-seam-with-named-pin or provably SAFE with the mechanism stated.

**A-677-2 (B2, the go-live transition — B2-i, PROVISIONAL for her bundle).** In the one render where a pane turns live while carrying a changed projection text, the pane completes its READ adoption before turning live. That is the read pane's own contract (projection-authoritative) finishing before liveness transfers — NOT the vetoed clean-buffer adoption of a live pane — and without it the race she is most likely to hit (clicking into a pane right after an MCP edit) re-admits the silent-overwrite class. Mark it PROVISIONAL in the record (adjacent to her "no clean-buffer adoption" words; recorded alternative B2-ii: leave the window as a residual); it joins the closeout bundle for her ratify.

**A-677-2 RATIFIED by the author, live, 2026-10-28 (relayed by the orchestrator; her words verbatim, her spelling as typed):** "I think taht what you're saying about going live in the same instant as a changed text showing it in a read pane going live is fine - that seems like a very edge scenario and it's still not two items editing the same live pane, so doesn't seem like it introduces the clobbering risk." — so B2-i builds as RULED behaviour (no longer PROVISIONAL) and drops from the closeout bundle; B2-ii stays recorded as the road not taken.

- **F-FR-1 — DISMISSED as the ruled table's recorded residual.** The gone table checks the NOTE for note/takes/lens/compare targets (the round-1 final call's own ruling); a dead block, lens or chapter inside a live note opens the note and the surface decides from its projection — the same accepted class as the compare dead-peer residual the final call recorded in one line.
